## Context

The operator requires an agent self-review and a hostile validation (HV) on every result, plus truthful, per-request audit in the MCP session log. Today:

- Reviews have no structured home. They live in free-text turns, `docs/receipts/hv/*` (integer `accuracy`/`completeness` 0-100), and the unlinked HostileReview subsystem (`double` 0-1). No score in the codebase is a lossless decimal.
- Turn logging is not trustworthy, so anything attached to a turn would attach to the wrong request. Receipts observed in this session (plugin 1.118.0):
  - Twin turns per prompt (e.g. `req-20261009T011653Z-prompt-b776` and `-c9a3`): MCP hooks were then registered twice (`~/.claude/settings.json` bridge hooks plus the enabled plugin `mcpserver@mcpserver-local` `hooks/hooks.json`). The bridge hooks were removed later on 2026-10-09 (MEMORY-FACT-003); a read-only check at 2026-10-09T17:54Z found no `hooks` key in `~/.claude/settings.json` or either `settings.local.json`, one plugin install, and one registration per event. The defect that let the duplicate go unreported remains: `validate-claude-mcp-hooks.ps1` wraps the `enabledPlugins` map as one item (line ~72), so it never sees the plugin as enabled and would report `valid` for a duplicate. A2 is therefore a regression fix verified against a fresh live inventory, not a cleanup of a current duplicate. Observed later the same day with hooks active: the prompt hook opens phantom turns for background task notifications, and each such open supersede-cancels the real turn in progress (`req-20261009T175347Z-prompt-dc63`, `req-20261009T175421Z-prompt-79dd`).
  - A new prompt cancels the in-progress turn: `Invoke-ReplSupersedeCurrentTurnIfInProgress` (`plugins/core/lib-ps/repl-invoke.ps1:2131`, called at `:2300`). Canceled while being worked: `6067`, `5360`, `bc34`, `8ab5`, `11dd`, `ea25`, `9a7d`, and the plan request `e18e`.
  - Background `<task-notification>` events open turns (`-986f`, `-06ab`, `-290b`, `-4ced`, `-695f`).
  - False durable-write receipt: `appendActions` on `req-20261009T005031Z-prompt-d3a5` returned `code: persisted, persisted: true`, but the server turn has 0 actions. The submit terminal guard `continue`s silently (`src/QBrainAi.Services/Services/SessionLogService.cs:1606-1618`) while the controller hard-codes `persisted = true` (`SessionLogController.cs:71-86`).
  - The stop gate auto-closes turns as `'Auto-closed by PowerShell stop gate.'` (`plugins/core/lib-ps/plugin-hook.ps1:1017`).
  - A single shared `current-turn.yaml` couples all turn edits, so edits to one request land on another.
  - Cross-session interference: this session's UserPromptSubmit hook reported `req-20261009T012640Z-prompt-b2ff` (a turn of a different ClaudeCode session, likely the Plan subagent's prompt) as "the root work turn"; hooks of subagents and parallel sessions share the workspace cache.
  - Hook-current turns can exist only locally (`req-20261009T013814Z-prompt-7ee0`: server PATCH returned "planFile is omitted", turn absent server-side).

Intended outcome: every user request is exactly one turn whose edits are independent of every other turn; every write receipt is truthful; every turn carries a `requestClass` and two review records (`agentReview`, `hostileReview`) with lossless decimal scores, a status lifecycle, and configurable pass thresholds; the server enforces completion rules; FR/TR/TEST/AC/UC are complete and traceable.

## Locked operator decisions (2026-10-09)

Turn logging
- Every user request (including messages queued mid-turn) is exactly one turn; actions and decisions map to the turn of the request that caused them, determined by the write-attribution rule below (D2). Enforcement of turn-logging correctness is Phase A and ships first.
- Plugin turn edits are independent: opening, editing, closing, or canceling one turn never changes another turn's local or server state; concurrent edits to different turns do not interfere.
- Unsupported-session failsafe (operator, 2026-10-10: "If QBrain is truly not available, use the failsafe already in the plugin." and, choosing between a failsafe capture and a warn-only Stop, "1"): when a host session fails closed under D6 (`unsupportedSince`), the model's session-log writes for a turn that was already open at that moment go to the plugin's existing failsafe instead of being refused (reviews excepted: they keep their no-failsafe rule and are recorded after the drain through the original pair), so the Stop gate's remedy stays accepted; the captured records drain at the next SessionStart through the trusted recovery flow with the turn's original pair (C5); every mint and every write for any other turn stays refused (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001).
- Clear-session closing rule (operator, 2026-10-10, choosing option A over leaving blocked and withdrawn turns open: "A"): clear-session completes each turn that meets the completion policy, fails each withdrawn turn (reason "withdrawn before delivery") and each turn the model marks blocked (with its blocker reason; `failed` requires `requestClass` only, FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-002-AC002), and leaves every other turn in progress, reported by requestId with its exact missing items, never fabricating reviews (TR-MCP-CLEARSESSION-001-AC001). Definition (catalog cross-reference, not a change to the decision): "marked blocked" means the turn's `blockers` section (the existing decision-list field, FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-006-AC001) holds at least one nonblank model-authored entry, read by the clear-session coordinator for that turn by its original (`sessionId`, `requestId`) pair, and the first entry is the fail reason; precedence per turn: withdrawn -> `failTurn` ("withdrawn before delivery"); else blocked -> `failTurn` with the blocker reason (blocked takes precedence over a ready completion); else meets the completion policy -> `completeTurn`; else stays in progress, reported with its exact missing items. Close prerequisites (catalog cross-reference, not a change to the decision): before clear-session fails a withdrawn or blocked turn, the model writes by that turn's original pair, within the same clear-session run and before its session-end step, every prerequisite the plugin and the server require for that close, in the Stop gate remedy order (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC009): a designDecision when the turn has no model evidence (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC002), then a `requestClass` when it is missing or `Unclassified`, judged from the turn's `queryText`; the coordinator then fails the turn with its reason (TR-MCP-CLEARSESSION-001-AC001).
- Request identity (operator D1, 2026-10-09: "have the server issue a request token when a message arrives"): the server mints every turn's `requestId` when a user message arrives (ingress), through the new mint operation `POST /qbrainai/sessionlog/{agent}/{sessionId}/mint`. The mint call carries an idempotency nonce derived from the host's own identity for that message event (host binding table): a host per-prompt id, or a SHA-256 of host event fields that every delivery of that event carries identically. Every delivery of one event therefore sends the same nonce and receives the same token, including network retries and failsafe replay on every host and, on hosts whose nonce is a stable per-message id (codex `turn_id`, grok and grok-bot `promptId`, cline-v2 `runId`, opencode `messageID`) or the bridge's single ordered source (claude-code, claude-cowork), two hook processes started by a doubly registered hook (on copilot and classic cline a second registration is refused before any mint, and a second new equal-field invocation fails closed); two separate user messages (including two intentionally identical prompts) are two events and receive two tokens. On copilot and classic cline, whose event fields include no per-message id, a message whose session, timestamp, and prompt all equal an already minted one is indistinguishable from a redelivery of it, so the host session fails closed under D6 instead of merging or guessing (host binding table, equal-field rule). The host id is only the idempotency key; the turn identity is the server-issued token. There is no prompt-text dedupe window. Every mint comes from a host arrival mechanism (a prompt hook, the arrival bridge, or a host-native hook adapter); no supported host relies on the model to mint. The stop gate's `duplicate_delivery_suspected` warning is a diagnostic only and is never counted as D1 compliance.
- Queued-message arrival (operator D5, 2026-10-09: "Arrival bridge"): a message the operator sends while a turn is running is minted when it is enqueued, not when the model later processes it. On hosts that record the enqueue in a transcript (Claude Code), a per-host-session arrival bridge started by the SessionStart hook tails the transcript and follows each queued message through the lifecycle the transcript records. Observed in this workspace's Claude Code transcripts on 2026-10-09: 117 `enqueue`, 79 `dequeue`, 35 `remove` (27 with reason `absorbed_mid_turn`), 2 `popAll`; a normal prompt is `enqueue` then `dequeue` within milliseconds, a mid-turn message is `enqueue` then `remove` with `absorbed_mid_turn` when it is injected into the running turn (for example the operator's mid-turn message beginning "write a global memory with the" was removed with `absorbed_mid_turn` at 2026-10-09T14:35:47.730Z), and `popAll` pulls queued text back into the input box. The bridge mints at `enqueue` (state `queued`); the request becomes `delivered` at `dequeue` (applied by the bridge from the transcript itself; on hosts with a bridge the prompt hook neither mints nor claims) or at `remove` with `absorbed_mid_turn`; it becomes `withdrawn` at `popAll` or a `remove` with any other or no reason. Task notifications and other system events that the host also enqueues are classified and never minted; on bridge hosts they keep their queue position (`kind: system`), so their `dequeue` never delivers a user request.
- Hosts without an arrival mechanism (operator D6, 2026-10-09: "Adapters or unsupported"): a host is supported for turn logging only through an arrival mechanism this plan specifies (a prompt hook that fires at enqueue, the claude-code/claude-cowork arrival bridge, or the classic-cline native-hook adapter); every other host is unsupported until a separately approved adapter exists, and a queue or enqueue event that P0.2 records on such a host becomes a traced follow-up adapter TODO (filed in P0.8) for operator approval (host binding table). A host with no arrival mechanism is declared unsupported for turn logging until it gains one: its plugin reports `turn_logging_unsupported` on every session-log verb and writes nothing (a host session that becomes unsupported after one of its requests was delivered follows the host binding table unsupported-session rule and the 2026-10-10 failsafe decision), and no first-sight minting is used anywhere.
- Plugin equivalence (operator D7, 2026-10-09, confirmed in chat "1 yes" for the requirement and its qualification posted by Codex in `MEMORY-PROCESS-007` at 17:48:48Z and 17:52:06Z): all QBrain.AI plugins keep equivalent functionality; shared capabilities, contracts, fixes, and behavior changes go to every plugin; host-specific adaptations preserve equivalent supported behavior; equivalence is limited by what each host's plugin infrastructure can support, no extraordinary effort is spent forcing unworkable equivalence, and each such limit is documented as a feature-specific capability limitation instead of being claimed as equivalent. A D6 `turn_logging_unsupported` host is such a documented limitation. The limitations live in `docs/AGENT-PLUGIN-FEATURE-MATRIX.md` (one row per plugin and feature: supported, adapted, or limited, with the reason and the receipt), first written in P0.8 from the P0.2 receipts, updated in A9 and S11, and checked by a docs contract test.
- Ninth plugin (operator D8, 2026-10-09: "2 yes"): `mcpserver-grok-bot-plugin` is in scope like the other eight.
- Write attribution (operator D2, 2026-10-09: tie each write to its request automatically through the agent app's hooks): every verb invocation is bound to one request automatically from host context, without the model naming it: the binding is the write target of the invoking host session at the moment the invocation starts, captured once and stored immutably with that invocation (its persist record and failsafe record), so a later message never retargets an invocation already started. A host session's write target is the request most recently delivered to the model in that host session: a prompt the host hands to the model as a new turn (the prompt hook on hosts without a bridge, or the bridge's `dequeue` delivery), or a queued message absorbed into the running turn (`absorbed_mid_turn`). Minting alone never moves the write target, so work the model is still doing for request R1 after R2 is enqueued stays on R1 until R2 is delivered. Explicit `requestId` remains accepted as an override, for example to continue R1 work after R2 was absorbed, and it is required while two or more delivered requests are open in the host session (D9). Attribution therefore maps each write to the request most recently delivered to the model in its host session when the write started. A write is never attributed by guesswork: when a verb cannot read its host session key from its own invocation context (host binding table), it binds only if exactly one live host session of its agent exists in the workspace (warning `host_session_inferred`); with none it is refused (`host_session_unknown`), and with more than one it is refused (`host_session_ambiguous`), in both cases unless it carries the trusted recovery or review-backfill pair (original MCP `sessionId` and `requestId`, verified against the server for this workspace and agent, C5); an explicit `requestId` alone never lets a verb without a host session key write. No verb ever targets the workspace-level `current-turn.yaml`.
- Ambiguous attribution (operator D9, 2026-10-09: "Refuse when ambiguous"): no host supplies a per-action signal of which request caused a write, so automatic binding applies only while exactly one delivered request is open in the host session. While two or more delivered requests are open (for example R2 absorbed mid-turn while R1 is still open), a write without an explicit `requestId` is refused with `request_ambiguous`, naming the open requests; the model passes `requestId` until all but one are closed. Writes are never attributed by guesswork.
- Copilot duplicate deliveries (operator D11, 2026-10-09: "Supported only if single hook"): Copilot gives no stable per-prompt id and separately registered hooks may see different `timestamp` values, so Copilot turn logging is supported only while the plugin's duplicate check proves exactly one MCP prompt hook across all Copilot hook sources; otherwise the Copilot prompt hook reports `turn_logging_unsupported` before any mint, and the condition is a D7 documented limitation until fixed. One Copilot prompt therefore never becomes two turns.
- Node runtime floor (operator D12, 2026-10-09: "Keep Node 18/20"): the Node core and the TypeScript hosts keep their declared engines (`plugins/core/lib-node/package.json` `node >=20`; mcpserver-cline-plugin, mcpserver-cline-v2-plugin, and mcpserver-opencode-plugin `>=18`; the Cline application's own `node >=22` is unaffected). Exact review scores are read with a raw-token parser in the Node core, not with `JSON.parse` reviver `context.source` (absent on Node 20, V8 11.3), and the parser is tested on Node 18, 20, 22, and the latest release.

Request class
- Turn scalar `requestClass`: `Code` | `Docs` | `Chore` (case-insensitive in, canonical PascalCase out, anything else rejected). Required, enforced at transition to `completed` or `failed`, not at creation (hooks create turns before the agent can classify). Legacy rows backfilled to `Unclassified` (readable; never accepted on new writes from agent surfaces).

Review record (shared by `agentReview` and `hostileReview`)
- Fields: `status` (`Unnecessary` | `Unstarted` | `Running` | `Pass` | `Fail` | `Other`), `otherNotes`, `correctness`, `completeness` (lossless decimal 0..1), `correctnessThreshold`, `completenessThreshold` (server-stamped, read-only), `reasons` (string[]), `reviewer`, `reviewedAt` (UTC), `requestJsonlPath`, `responseJsonlPath`. No `passed` bool.
- One record per kind per turn; latest write wins.
- Server creates both at turn creation as `Unstarted`. `hostileReview` flips to `Unnecessary` when `requestClass` becomes `Chore`, and back to `Unstarted` if the class changes away while still `Unnecessary`. Records past `Unstarted`/`Unnecessary` are never auto-changed. Legacy turns are backfilled `agentReview=Unstarted`, `hostileReview=Unstarted`. (Catalog cross-references, not a change to the decision: the flip and its reverse apply only to in-progress turns, FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-006-AC002 and UC-4; a reset restores the creation default for the turn's current class, FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC004.)
- `otherNotes` with text is rejected unless `status` is `Other`.
- Strict per-status fields: `Unnecessary`/`Unstarted` carry no scores, reasons, reviewer, timestamps, or paths; `Running` requires `reviewer`, forbids scores; `Pass`/`Fail` require `correctness`, `completeness`, `reviewer`, `reviewedAt`; `hostileReview` `Pass`/`Fail` also require `requestJsonlPath` and `responseJsonlPath`; `Other` requires non-empty `otherNotes` (the Other reason; operator D4, 2026-10-09: "Other requires Other Reason be populated") and allows optional scores, `reasons`, `reviewer`, `reviewedAt`, and receipt paths. `Unnecessary` is server-managed only.
- Thresholds: `Mcp:SessionLog:ReviewThresholds:{Agent|Hostile}:{Correctness|Completeness}` (decimal 0..1, per instance through `McpInstanceResolver` overrides), default 0.98, inclusive (score >= threshold passes). Pass requires both scores to meet their thresholds. The server derives `Pass`/`Fail`; a supplied `Pass`/`Fail` that contradicts the derivation is rejected (422 `review_status_conflict`); omitting status lets the server derive it. `Other` is never derived. Thresholds in force are stamped on the record.
- Names stay `correctness`/`completeness`. HV tooling maps `accuracy` -> `correctness` and integer percent -> decimal/100.

Completion rules
- `completed` requires `requestClass` plus `agentReview.status` in `Pass`/`Fail`/`Other`; for `Code`/`Docs` also `hostileReview.status` in `Pass`/`Fail`/`Other`; for `Chore` `hostileReview` is not checked (HV not required). `Unstarted`/`Running` block where checked. Pass is not required (Fail/Other complete).
- `failed` requires `requestClass` only. Reviews remain writable on terminal turns.
- Server-internal writers (QuadBrain chat, AuditedAgentCliClient, WorkspacePolicyService, federation replication, transcript/session ingestion) are exempt by code-path origin; they never fabricate reviews.

Scope and process
- Server + client + REPL + MCP tools + plugin core, and every agent plugin updated and tested as needed (matrix below).
- Strict BDPv4 per slice (below). Complete FR/TR/TEST with ACs and Use Cases (UC) for every new FR.
- Hostile validation runs on Codex CLI with model `gpt-6-sol` at reasoning effort `xhigh`, launched by the implementing agent at the end of every slice once all of that slice's gates are green (operator D10, 2026-10-09: "Only HV when all green at end of slice"), and at P0's end (HV-P0); the closing slices A9 and S11 also carry the phase-wide HV-A and HV-B. This replaces the earlier PR-comment HV protocol (operator, 2026-10-09: "Yes, replace the PR-comment HV with Codex gpt-6-sol xhigh").
- Licensing (operator, 2026-10-09: "preserve license on existing files, Apache 2.0 for new files."): existing files and existing package declarations keep their license unchanged (root `LICENSE` Apache-2.0; existing `PackageLicenseExpression` MIT values; `plugins/core/lib-node/package.json` MIT); every file this plan creates in McpServer or a plugin repo is Apache-2.0 and, where the file type allows comments, starts with an `SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0` comment; any new package or artifact manifest declares Apache-2.0. P0 and each slice's self-review record `git diff --name-only --diff-filter=A <base>..HEAD` per changed repo with, per file, its SPDX line or "no comment syntax", and any added commentable file without `SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0` is a FAIL claim in that HV (triage `triage-report-8509a6345e6c408a981ade489513fa2b` records the pre-existing MIT/Apache mismatch).

This is the marker's documented bootstrap exception to the plugin-only rule (`AGENTS-README-FIRST.yaml` lines 196 and 204: plugin acquisition through the authenticated tool-registry endpoints); it is not used for session, TODO, requirements, or traceability operations.

Host binding table (locks the D1 nonce source, the D5 arrival source, and the D2 host session key per host; every turn token is still minted by the server). Rules common to all hosts:
- Nonce: computed from the event itself, never from the hook process, so every delivery of one event yields the same nonce; a value outside `^[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,64}$` is replaced by the 64 lowercase hex characters of its SHA-256. Equal-field rule (copilot and classic cline, whose nonce hashes event fields with no per-message id): under `turn-open.lock`, a new prompt-hook invocation whose nonce already has a failsafe mint record or a `pending-<nonce>` turn in that host session, or whose mint returns `merged`, cannot be told apart from a separate message with equal session, timestamp, and prompt, so it mints and binds nothing and the host session fails closed under D6 exactly as the bridge does for an unattributable record (`unsupportedSince: { causeNonce, causeOperation: prompt_hook, reason: indistinguishable_arrival }` with the session-level envelope; every session-log verb then reports `turn_logging_unsupported` and writes nothing to the server until the session ends, except that writes for a turn already open at that moment go to the plugin failsafe under the unsupported-session failsafe rule, FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001; its open turns are listed at the next SessionStart). A network retry or failsafe replay of a stored mint record is a drain of that record, not a new invocation, and reuses its nonce without failing closed.
- Unsupported-session rule (D6; operator option 1 of 2026-10-10): every check that makes a host session report `turn_logging_unsupported` after one of its requests was delivered records `unsupportedSince` with the session-level envelope in `turn-state.yaml` in the same atomic replace that makes the session unsupported, with `reason` set to that check's code: `queue_removal_ambiguous` (bridge), `indistinguishable_arrival` (equal-field rule), `arrival_bridge_format_unrecognized` (an unknown queue operation, a record missing a required field, or a transcript shorter than `appliedThroughOffset`), `duplicate_prompt_hook` (the copilot or classic-cline single-registration check starts failing; the first verb or hook in that host session that observes it records it), or, for any other D6 check, its own reason code; plus `causeOffset` or `causeNonce` where the cause has one, else `detectedAtUtc`. Whatever the reason, the unsupported-session failsafe of FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001 then applies: writes for a turn of that host session still in progress when `unsupportedSince` was recorded (a delivered open request, or a `withdrawn` queue entry still `in_progress`, addressed by its original pair; a minted request still `queued` is not covered and stays a warning) are captured, and every mint and every other write is refused. The state lasts until the host session ends, or until a SessionStart clears its cause where the cause's own rule allows (the bridge restarting on a valid transcript); the captured records drain at that SessionStart. A host that is unsupported from its first event mints nothing, has no open turn, and records no `unsupportedSince`.
- P0.2 gating rule (D6, D7): every case, gate, or live assertion that needs a host's arrival mechanism or a P0.2-probed host field runs only when that host's P0.2 row passed; when the row failed or was unavailable, the host's D6 case `<Host>_P0_2NotPassed_TurnLoggingUnsupported_ZeroMintsZeroWrites` runs instead in the same slice (A3: `ClaudeCode_P0_2NotPassed_TurnLoggingUnsupported_ZeroMintsZeroWrites`, `Grok_P0_2NotPassed_TurnLoggingUnsupported_ZeroMintsZeroWrites`, `GrokBot_P0_2NotPassed_TurnLoggingUnsupported_ZeroMintsZeroWrites`, `Copilot_P0_2NotPassed_TurnLoggingUnsupported_ZeroMintsZeroWrites`, `Cowork_P0_2NotPassed_TurnLoggingUnsupported_ZeroMintsZeroWrites`; A8: `Cline_P0_2NotPassed_TurnLoggingUnsupported_ZeroMintsZeroWrites`, `ClineV2_P0_2NotPassed_TurnLoggingUnsupported_ZeroMintsZeroWrites`, `OpenCode_P0_2NotPassed_TurnLoggingUnsupported_ZeroMintsZeroWrites`), and every live check excludes that host with its P0.2 receipt (codex excepted: a failed or unavailable codex row, or a codex enqueue-versus-processing result of "only when processed", stops P0 instead, because every HV reviewer persists its own turn through the Codex plugin; host binding table, codex). The P0.2 outcome that selected each branch is that host's feature-matrix receipt. The Cowork bridge fixtures of A3, the per-host mint cases of A3 (codex, grok, grok-bot, copilot) and A8 (classic cline, cline-v2, opencode), the copilot duplicate-hook transition of A9, TEST-MCP-PLUGIN-013-AC001, the codex live assertion, and the Phase A live check on claude-code are instances of this rule.
- Host session key: read from the verb's own invocation context as listed per host. Long-lived processes (stdio MCP servers, the arrival bridge itself) never trust a key captured at spawn time, because a host may change the session in place (Claude Code `/clear`; resume without an explicit id keeps the startup id in long-lived subprocesses per the Claude Code env-var reference); they resolve the key per call by the live-session rule.
- Queued-message arrival on every host (D5, D6): P0.2 records, per host, whether a message sent while a turn is running reaches an arrival mechanism at enqueue. Where the prompt event itself fires at enqueue for a message the host takes into the running turn, it is the arrival mechanism; a prompt event that fires at enqueue for a message the host runs as its own later turn is not, because a prompt-hook mint is delivered at once (TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001 AC-005) and would move the write target and the stop ledger to a request the model has not received (D2; FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001), so P0.2 records that result as only when processed. The only queue adapter this plan specifies is the claude-code/claude-cowork arrival bridge. For every other host whose P0.2 result is only when processed (under the rule above) (codex excepted: that result stops P0, because every HV reviewer persists its own turn through the Codex plugin), the host is unsupported for turn logging in this plan (`turn_logging_unsupported`, D6), whether or not it has a separate enqueue or message-queue event; when it has one, P0.2 records that event's observed payload, and P0.8 files a traced follow-up TODO proposing the host's adapter (payload, nonce, correlation, lifecycle, replay, tests) for operator approval; until that adapter is specified, approved, and delivered, the host stays `turn_logging_unsupported` in this plan and its `<Host>_P0_2NotPassed_TurnLoggingUnsupported_ZeroMintsZeroWrites` case applies.
- Live-session rule (D2 fallback): a host session is live from its first mint until its session-end event (Claude Code `SessionEnd`, including reason `clear`; Codex `SessionEnd`; Copilot session end; cline-v2 and opencode session close) marks `sessions/<key>/ended.yaml`, or until 12 hours without a mint or verb (the idle rule writes `ended.yaml` with `reason: idle`; a session-end event writes `reason: session_end`). A verb without its own key binds to the single live session of its agent in the workspace (`host_session_inferred`), and is refused with `host_session_unknown` (none live) or `host_session_ambiguous` (several live) unless it passes the trusted (MCP `sessionId`, `requestId`) pair of C5.
- claude-code: nonce: the bridge's `q-` enqueue nonce (the bridge is the only minter, FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC009); hook key payload `session_id`; verb key env `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` read at verb start in shell, PowerShell, and hook subprocesses (equals `session_id`; those subprocesses see the updated value after `/clear`); stdio MCP verbs use the live-session rule; payloads carrying `agent_id` are subagent events (system events, no turn); the subagent's own Bash and PowerShell verbs carry the parent's `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` and bind to the parent's write target; stop uses payload `stop_hook_active`. Arrival bridge (D5), the only minter on claude-code (known-good single source of truth: the host transcript is one ordered log written by one producer, so the bridge needs no key shared with the hook, which the host does not provide: Claude Code's `queue-operation` records carry no `promptId` although its user records do): started by SessionStart with the payload `transcript_path`, it applies `queue-operation` lines in file order. Every `enqueue` takes a queue position, so FIFO `dequeue` stays exact. An `enqueue` whose `content` the system-event classifier marks as a system event is added as `{ kind: system, enqueueOffset, enqueueTimestamp, promptSha256, state: queued }` with the session-level envelope `{ workspacePath, agent, mcpSessionId, hostSessionKey }` (it has no nonce or requestId, and none is ever invented for it) and is never minted. An `enqueue` whose `content` the classifier accepts as a user request: mint with nonce `q-` + SHA-256(`session_id` LF the record's starting byte offset in the transcript LF enqueue `timestamp` LF `content`) truncated to 62 hex characters (the offset is unique per record and identical on replay, so two distinct records with equal timestamp and content get two nonces), and add `{ kind: request, requestId, nonce, enqueueOffset, enqueueTimestamp, promptSha256, state: queued, stateOffset, mintedAtUtc }` with the root-id envelope to the queue in `sessions/<key>/turn-state.yaml`. `dequeue` (no content) removes the head of the host session's `queued` entries: a `kind: request` head becomes `delivered` and the write target; a `kind: system` head is removed and delivers nothing. `remove` and `popAll` records that match a `kind: system` entry drop it without a turn. `remove` with reason `absorbed_mid_turn`: the `queued` entry whose `promptSha256` equals the record's `content` hash (both written by the same producer) becomes `delivered` and the write target when exactly one queued entry has that hash. `remove` with any other or no reason: the matching `queued` entry becomes `withdrawn` when exactly one queued entry has that content hash. `popAll`: when, for every content line it carries, the number of lines with that content equals the number of queued entries with that content hash, those entries become `withdrawn`. Fail-closed rule (D6): a `remove` carries `commandUuid` and `deliveryId` (Claude Code 2.1.293), which also appear on the delivered message's `queued_command` attachment, but `enqueue` and `dequeue` carry no key shared with it, so a `remove` that matches two or more queued entries, or a `popAll` that carries fewer lines of a content than there are queued entries with that content, cannot be attributed (a `popAll` whose line count equals the matching entry count withdraws them all, because every candidate is withdrawn). The bridge never guesses: at the first such record it stops before it without advancing `appliedThroughOffset`, sets `turn-state.yaml` `unsupportedSince: { causeOffset, causeOperation, reason: queue_removal_ambiguous }`, and from then on every session-log verb in that host session reports `turn_logging_unsupported` and writes nothing to the server until the session ends, except that writes for a turn already open at that moment go to the plugin failsafe (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001). Turns that are open at that moment are left exactly as they are on the server, the stop gate blocks on each only until the model's close of it is captured in the plugin failsafe and then warns `queue_removal_ambiguous` listing them (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001), and the next SessionStart for the agent lists them with their original (MCP `sessionId`, `requestId`) pairs for closing through the trusted recovery flow (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC006). P0.2 records the `remove` identity fields; the bridge never uses them to pair with an `enqueue`. Only `queue-operation` lines are arrival records; every other record type is ignored by design. A `queue-operation` with an unknown operation or a missing required field is never skipped: the bridge stops before it without advancing `appliedThroughOffset`, records warning `arrival_bridge_format_unrecognized` and, after a delivered request, `unsupportedSince` with that reason and the record's `causeOffset` in the same atomic replace (unsupported-session rule), and the host session reports `turn_logging_unsupported` on every session-log verb (D6) until a verified adapter handles that shape, while writes for a turn already open go to the plugin failsafe (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001). Each record's state change and the advance of `appliedThroughOffset` past it are one atomic replace of `turn-state.yaml` (temp file, flush, rename, under `turn-open.lock`); records that start below `appliedThroughOffset` are skipped, so a restart never re-applies a record; the server mint for `enqueue` happens before that replace and is idempotent (same nonce, `merged`). Records are applied by whichever process holds `turn-open.lock`: the bridge, or a session-log verb, which first records the transcript length at the moment the invocation starts and, after taking the lock, applies every pending record that ends at or before that length, never a record appended later, before it binds (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC006), so a verb started after a `dequeue` line was written binds to that request, and a `dequeue` appended after the verb started never retargets it (D2). The prompt hook on claude-code and claude-cowork neither mints nor claims; it classifies system events and starts no turn. P0.2 verifies that every user prompt (in each prompt form of FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC009) produces an `enqueue` record and that `dequeue` always takes the head; if either fails, claude-code is `turn_logging_unsupported` (D6) until an adapter with a traceable key exists, recorded in the feature matrix.
- claude-cowork: same fields and arrival bridge as claude-code. P0.2 verifies that Cowork executes plugin hooks; if it does not, Cowork has no arrival mechanism and is unsupported for turn logging under D6 (`turn_logging_unsupported`, nothing written) until it gains one. A9 repairs the dangling `bin/mcpserver-stdio.cmd` connector and adopts the bridge for Cowork only when its P0.2 bridge probes pass, with the Cowork RED fixtures of A3 (an instance of the P0.2 gating rule above).
- codex: A9 adds `hooks/hooks.json` (SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, PostToolUse, Stop, SessionEnd per the Codex hooks reference) and, until the hook trust check below passes, codex reports `turn_logging_unsupported` on every session-log verb (D6) instead of the manual steps in `skills/enforcement/SKILL.md`, which have the model run the prompt hook itself and are removed in A9; nonce payload `turn_id`; hook key payload `session_id` (subagents share the parent's); verb key env `CODEX_THREAD_ID` only if P0.2 proves it equals `session_id` in shell commands, else the live-session rule. Codex skips plugin hooks until the operator trusts that hook definition (OpenAI Codex plugin documentation), so A9 and the rollout include an operator trust step, and the Phase A live check (after the elevated deploy and the branch REPL install) includes the live assertion that a fresh Codex prompt, launched through the exact HV launch form of the hostile validation protocol (`codex exec -m gpt-6-sol -c model_reasoning_effort="xhigh" ... --json -`, prompt on stdin), produces a hook-minted server turn, and that a message sent with `codex queue` while a turn runs in an interactive session on the shared app-server daemon produces exactly one hook-minted turn of its own (both parts are the codex live assertion); codex stays `turn_logging_unsupported` until that assertion passes, and it runs only when codex's P0.2 row passed (P0.2 gating rule). Because every HV reviewer must persist its own turn through the Codex plugin, a codex P0.2 row that fails or is unavailable does not select the D6 fallback: P0 stops and the operator is shown the receipt.
- copilot: A9 points `plugin.json` at `hooks/hooks.json` and removes the dangling `.sh` hook file; nonce `cp-` + SHA-256(`sessionId` LF `timestamp` LF `prompt`) truncated to 61 hex characters (no per-message id exists, so the equal-field rule applies), from the UserPromptSubmit payload fields the Copilot CLI hooks reference documents (`sessionId`, `timestamp` in Unix milliseconds, `cwd`, `prompt`; VS Code form `session_id`, ISO `timestamp`); hook key `sessionId`/`session_id`; verbs use the live-session rule (no session env var is documented). The reference does not promise that two separately registered hook commands receive an identical `timestamp`, so the Copilot adoption also enforces a single delivery boundary: an A2-equivalent check enumerates every source the Copilot hooks reference documents (policy `/etc/github-copilot/policy.d/*.json`; repository `.github/hooks/*.json`; user `~/.copilot/hooks/` honoring `COPILOT_HOME`; inline hooks in `.github/copilot/settings.json`, `.github/copilot/settings.local.json`, and the cross-tool `.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/settings.local.json`; `~/.copilot/settings.json`; plugin `hooks.json` or `hooks/hooks.json`; all sources run, and the Copilot cloud agent reads only `.github/hooks/*.json`), detects duplicate MCP prompt hooks across all of them, repairs the duplicates it owns (plugin, repository, and user files, with a backup), and for a duplicate it cannot remove (a policy file) fails closed: Copilot turn logging reports `turn_logging_unsupported` with operator remediation text and, after a delivered request, records `unsupportedSince` with reason `duplicate_prompt_hook` (`DuplicateHookAfterDelivery_UnsupportedSince_OpenTurnCloseCaptured`), recorded as a D7 documented limitation; P0.2 captures two real deliveries of one prompt from two registered hooks when the Copilot CLI is available and records whether their `timestamp` values match (evidence only; D11 does not depend on it); A9 tests duplicate detection with one fixture per source, that with exactly one MCP prompt hook a delivery mints one turn, that two arrivals with equal prompt and different `timestamp` mint two turns, that a second invocation with equal session, timestamp, and prompt after the first has minted mints nothing and fails the host session closed with `indistinguishable_arrival` and zero later writes, and that a mint retry or failsafe replay reuses its nonce without failing closed (`Copilot_SamePromptDifferentTimestamp_TwoMints`, `Copilot_EqualFieldsSecondInvocation_FailsClosed_NoSecondMint`, `Copilot_MintRetryAndFailsafeReplay_ReuseNonce_NoFailClose`), and that with two MCP prompt hooks registered (in any pair of sources, including two deliveries that differ only in `timestamp`) the prompt hook reports `turn_logging_unsupported` before any mint and the server receives zero mints (D11).
- grok: the core reads camelCase keys when the host is grok: nonce `promptId`, hook key `sessionId`, stop `stopHookActive`, subagent `subagentType`; verb key env `GROK_SESSION_ID` only if P0.2 proves it is set for shell commands, else the live-session rule. Grok's default execution of `~/.claude/settings.json` hooks is a duplicate-registration source that A2's validator reports; with `promptId` as nonce, such duplicates still mint one turn.
- grok-bot: same payload handling as grok (camelCase `promptId` nonce, `sessionId` hook key, `stopHookActive`, `subagentType`) until P0.2 records the Grok Bot cloud runtime's actual hook payload, session environment for shell commands, and queued-message behavior; each P0.2 outcome selects among the rules above (prompt-hook arrival at enqueue, or `turn_logging_unsupported` with a follow-up adapter TODO for a recorded queue event, or a documented capability limitation per D7); verbs use `GROK_SESSION_ID` only if P0.2 proves it is set in the cloud runtime, else the live-session rule.
- cline (classic): the plugin itself is a stdio MCP server with no hooks, but the Cline app has native hooks (UserPromptSubmit with `prompt`; common fields `taskId`, `hookName`, `timestamp`, `workspaceRoots`, `userId`, `model`; seen in the local Cline source `/home/sharpninja/github/cline` at `b090c4a08`, `apps/vscode/src/core/hooks/hook-factory.ts:102-120,201-228` and `apps/vscode/proto/cline/hooks.proto:44-62`, not in public docs). Arrival adapter (D6): the cline plugin repo ships a Cline UserPromptSubmit hook script that mints through the Node core with nonce `cl-` + SHA-256(`taskId` LF `timestamp` LF `prompt`) truncated to 61 hex characters (the hook factory sets `timestamp` from `Date.now()` when the hook fires and no per-message id exists, so the equal-field rule applies, tested in A8 and in the cline repo's G6 suite in A9: `Cline_SamePromptDifferentTimestamp_TwoMints`, `Cline_EqualFieldsSecondInvocation_FailsClosed_NoSecondMint`, `Cline_MintRetryAndFailsafeReplay_ReuseNonce_NoFailClose`) and host session key `taskId`; the MCP server's tools carry no `taskId`, so they use the live-session rule; single registration is enforced as for copilot. P0.2 verifies in the installed Cline build that native hooks run, where hook scripts are installed, the payload fields, and whether queued messages fire the hook at arrival; if no arrival mechanism exists, classic Cline is unsupported for turn logging under D6 (`turn_logging_unsupported`).
- cline-v2: turn opens in `beforeRun` with nonce `runId`; host session key `conversationId` (lifecycle snapshot and tool context; the plugin stops stripping `sessionId`, `conversationId`, `runId`, `toolCallId` at `plugin.ts:122-140`); prompt text from the last user entry of `snapshot.messages`; stop-equivalent `afterRun` (no per-run session close); `@cline/core` pin raised to the version shipping `@cline/shared` 0.0.92 types. P0.2 records whether a queued message starts its own run at arrival; if it does not, cline-v2 is unsupported for turn logging in this plan under D6 (`turn_logging_unsupported`), and if a message-queue event exists, P0.2 records that event's observed payload, and P0.8 files a traced follow-up TODO proposing the host's adapter (payload, nonce, correlation, lifecycle, replay, tests) for operator approval; until that adapter is specified, approved, and delivered, the host stays `turn_logging_unsupported` in this plan and its `<Host>_P0_2NotPassed_TurnLoggingUnsupported_ZeroMintsZeroWrites` case applies.
- opencode: turn opens in `chat.message` for user messages with nonce `messageID` (fired when the message is created; that is arrival only under the queued-message arrival rule above, which P0.2 (i) checks); host session key `sessionID`; plugin tools read `_context.sessionID`, and shell commands get `MCP_HOST_SESSION_KEY` from the `shell.env` hook; stop-equivalent `session.idle`; the start/end regexes and the factory signature are replaced.

P0 per plugin: fetch origin; resolve the remote default branch with `git ls-remote --symref origin HEAD` (`main` for eight repos, `master` for opencode on 2026-10-09); compare local uncommitted work against origin lineage (`CORE-MANIFEST.yaml` coreVersion ancestry, EOL-insensitive diff); preserve any local work in a uniquely tagged stash (record SHA); fast-forward to that default branch; record version and head; re-check every row of the host binding table against the head and record any difference as a RequirementRefined decision before A1. Never discard uncommitted work without that receipt.

Per-plugin changes:
- PowerShell hosts (claude-code, claude-cowork, codex, copilot, grok, grok-bot): receive all plugin-core changes through `./build.ps1 SyncAgentPlugins --agent-plugin-parent /home/sharpninja/github --plugin-version <version>` (A9 with 1.119.0; S8 and S9 with 1.120.0) plus host wrappers from `hooks-templates/generate-wrappers.ps1`; host-specific glue exactly as the host binding table states (nonce source, hook and verb host-session keys, subagent and system-event markers, stop field); the A9 host-repo repairs named there (codex `hooks/hooks.json`, copilot hook path, cowork connector, and each host's manifest and hook registration); the core reads land in `plugins/core/lib-ps` with their first consumer and reach the hosts through the sync: the per-host nonce sources (codex `turn_id`, grok and grok-bot `promptId`, copilot `cp-`), the subagent markers (`agent_id`, `subagentType`, `MCP_SUBAGENT_ID`/`GROK_SUBAGENT_ID`), and the arrival-bridge launch from SessionStart in A3; the `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` verb key, the verb-key env reads P0.2 selected (`CODEX_THREAD_ID` for codex, `GROK_SESSION_ID` for grok and grok-bot, each only when its P0.2 row selected it, else that host's verbs use the live-session rule), the grok `sessionId` hook key, and the SessionEnd ended-marker writer in A4; the `stop_hook_active`/`stopHookActive` reads in A7; duplicate-registration detection where the host can double-register hooks (A2 equivalent; Grok's default run of `~/.claude/settings.json` hooks included); skill/README docs for recordReview, requestClass, automatic binding with optional requestId override.
- TypeScript hosts (cline, cline-v2, opencode): all shared behavior changes land once in `plugins/core/lib-node` (`src/tools/session.ts`, `src/tools/session-shim.ts`, `src/cache/cache-manager.ts`, `src/runtime/host-context.ts`, `src/transport/*`) in slices A8 and S9, with jest tests in `plugins/core/lib-node/tests` (`session-shim.test.ts`, `cache-manager.test.ts`, `host-context.test.ts`, `repl-bridge.test.ts`, plus new suites); A8 is the Node core only, with host behavior exercised in core jest through recorded host payload fixtures. Then each plugin adopts the new packed core and adds the host glue the host binding table fixes (in A9 for the Phase A glue, after A9's pack-name fix, the Node core 0.3.0 bump, and the sync; in S9 for the review surfaces): cline-v2 (`beforeRun` mint with `runId`, `conversationId` key, `afterRun` stop-equivalent, no per-run session close, `@cline/core` pin raised), opencode (`chat.message` mint with `messageID`, `sessionID` key via `_context` and `shell.env`, `session.idle` stop-equivalent, regex and factory replaced), cline (Cline native UserPromptSubmit hook adapter with `taskId` key and derived nonce, MCP tools on the live-session rule, or `turn_logging_unsupported` per D6); each exposes `requestClass`, `session_record_review`, the `requestId` override (required under D9 while two or more delivered requests are open), truthful outcomes, decisions/requirements/blockers.
- Every plugin: version bump, `CORE-MANIFEST.yaml` or packed-core version pin (where applicable), plugin-owned docs/skills/ENFORCEMENT.md and their tests updated; marker `agent_plugins` versions verified after server restart. Installed equals synced: a sync changes only the plugin checkouts (each slice's G6 tests those checkouts), while each host runs the copy it installed: Codex the copy its Git marketplace installed (`[marketplaces.mcpserver-codex-plugin]`, source `https://github.com/sharpninja/mcpserver-codex-plugin.git`, cached under `~/.codex/plugins/cache/mcpserver-codex-plugin/mcpserver/<version>`), and Claude Code the copy cached from its directory marketplace (`mcpserver@mcpserver-local` from `/home/sharpninja/github/mcpserver-claude-code-plugin`, under `~/.claude/plugins/cache/mcpserver-local/mcpserver/<version>`). A host's installed copy is refreshed only after the server its synced plugins call is deployed (plugins never ship ahead of the server they call): in the Phase A live check after its deploy (from the pushed A9 plugin branches, 1.119.0), in the Phase B live check after its deploy (from the pushed S8/S9 plugin branches, 1.120.0), and in each post-merge rollout after its deploy (from the merged default branches). Between those points every host keeps its installed copy, so the HV-A1..HV-A8 reviewers use the pre-plan Codex install, the HV-S1..HV-S10 reviewers the merged 1.119.0 install (neither needs a class or review, because the S10 gate is not deployed), and the implementing host's 1.120.0 verbs never run against a server without review records. The refresh covers every host whose live check, live smoke, or HV reviewer runs on this machine, through that host's plugin manager, and removes before it installs, because an install of the same version (as after a merge) may not replace the cached copy (Codex: `codex plugin marketplace remove mcpserver-codex-plugin`, `codex plugin marketplace add https://github.com/sharpninja/mcpserver-codex-plugin.git --ref <branch>`, `codex plugin remove mcpserver@mcpserver-codex-plugin`, `codex plugin add mcpserver@mcpserver-codex-plugin`, then `codex app-server daemon restart` so daemon-hosted sessions load the refreshed copy (run only with operator approval, because it restarts the server that hosts the interactive Codex sessions on this machine); Claude Code: `claude plugin marketplace update mcpserver-local`, `claude plugin uninstall --keep-data mcpserver@mcpserver-local`, `claude plugin install mcpserver@mcpserver-local`, then a restart; for a host the sync installs directly, such as cline-v2 under `~/.cline/plugins/_installed/local`, the sync is that step). Its receipt records per host that the installed version and revision equal the sync receipt's version and the branch head and that the installed `lib/plugin-hook.ps1` (codex also `hooks/hooks.json`; a TypeScript host its packed core) has the synced SHA-256. No trust step, live check, live assertion, or HV launch that depends on a refresh proceeds without this receipt.

Per-plugin test gate (G6, run for every plugin touched by a slice; zero failures, zero skips):
- PowerShell hosts: `pwsh -NoProfile -NonInteractive -Command "Invoke-Pester -Path tests -CI"` and `bats tests/*.bats`.
- Node core: in `plugins/core/lib-node` `npm ci; npm run build; npm test` (jest), run on each of Node 18, 20, 22, and the latest release (D12 version matrix, through the version manager the host provides, for example `mise exec node@20 -- npm test`); the TypeScript hosts run their suites on their lowest declared engine and on the latest release.
- TypeScript hosts (cline, cline-v2, opencode): `npm ci; npm run build; npm test` in each repo.
- `./build.ps1 PluginSessionLogIntegration` for hosts it covers.
- Phase exits require G6 green for all nine plugins and the Node core.

Shared contracts (as agreed in `MEMORY-PROCESS-007`; P0.7 posts this consolidated text as a CONTRACT entry):
- C1 Multiple open turns per host session: a map of open turns by requestId plus a per-host-session write target (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC005, FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-003, FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007); no single current-turn slot; "finish before another starts" applies per turn, never per session. D9 takes precedence over any latest-delivered fallback: automatic binding applies only while exactly one delivered request is open in the host session, otherwise an unqualified write is refused with `request_ambiguous`; minting alone never moves the write target.
- C2 Server-minted turn identity (alias journal: A3/A5 own it in the PowerShell cache, A8 in the Node cache, and A8 keeps committed aliases durably discoverable to QB-AGENT-001's outbox, which reapplies them idempotently until it has durably applied them (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC008); its observable contract is FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC008, and its interface is locked in TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008 AC-007): requestIds come from the mint route (D1); an event recorded before the mint returns carries the turn's `ingressNonce` and the provisional `pending-<nonce>` id. The move from `pending-<nonce>` to the minted requestId is the single explicit exception to requestId immutability and never changes the logical owning turn: the alias (old id, new id, `aliasedAtUtc`) is journaled atomically before any event is rewritten, every bound event is rebound in original order (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC008), readers resolve either id through the alias journal's permanent lookup entry, which is never pruned (only the consumer-delivery obligation is retired once every required consumer acknowledges), and restart and replay reapply a journaled alias without minting again.
- C3 Turn outcomes and event persistence receipts stay separate (TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008 AC-006). Turn outcomes (server per turn: `created`, `merged`, `skipped_terminal`; no `skipped_duplicate`, removed by the operator on 2026-10-09) are owned by this plan; event receipts (eventId, payloadHash, committed revision) by QB-AGENT-001. On a Node persistence path with an audit outbox: `created` and `merged` (including reason `no_change`) count as persisted only when the event identity, hash, and committed revision match; `outcomeVerified:false` (server without per-turn outcomes) never satisfies a mandatory persistence obligation; `skipped_terminal` maps to `rejected`, is never retried or quarantined, and keeps the event and its receipt (reason, `storedStatus`) as terminal evidence, while only the plugin failsafe record is cleared.
- C4 Every persisted or queued event carries its immutable D2 binding `{ hostSessionKey, keySource, ingressNonce, requestId, boundAtUtc }`, captured once; replay always uses the captured binding and never re-resolves it; apart from the C2 alias, eventId, order, payloadJson, and payloadHash never change.
- C5 State is scoped per host session key (host binding table), not per process; one process may serve several host sessions (cline-v2 conversations); REPL ownership may stay per process. An explicit requestId resolves to exactly one immutable owning MCP session within the validated workspace and agent; ordinary host audit writes remain bound to their host-owned conversation context; explicit recovery and review-backfill operations (including the D10 slice-verdict backfill) may target an earlier host session only with its original (MCP `sessionId`, `requestId`) pair, selected through the trusted host or recovery flow.
- C6 Action types come only from `docs/context/action-types.md`. QB-AGENT-001 owns adding `tool_execution` (its requirement, the doc change, server validation, and FR/TR/TEST coverage) and no component emits it before that lands; code in this plan records tool runs as dialog items with category `tool_call`/`tool_result`, and its tests accept `tool_execution` once it is documented.
- C7 Each decision is written as the workspace triple: a `designDecisions` entry, a dialog item with category `decision`, and a `design_decision` action, with idempotent replay (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-006-AC004; A6 for PowerShell, A8 for the Node core).
- C8 Completion: after S10 a completion without `requestClass` and terminal reviews is refused with 400 `session_turn_completion_gate`; the refusal is final for that submission attempt, not for the turn, which stays in progress for corrected review data; the same invalid submission is never retried automatically and its audit evidence is preserved (the plugins write carried evidence in a verified additive write before the status transition, FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-004-AC003); hooks never complete turns; `Other` "pending slice HV" is never an AGREE. QB-AGENT-001's governed completion additionally stays blocked while retained mandatory `skipped_terminal`/`rejected` evidence exists, even with zero pending and quarantine counts; clearing the plugin failsafe does not clear that obligation, and this plan's gates neither weaken nor duplicate that rule.
- C9 Typed failures carry the server or plugin detail code (`turn_immutable`, `skipped_terminal`, `host_session_unknown`, `host_session_ambiguous`, `request_ambiguous`, `request_unknown`, `turn_logging_unsupported`, `mint_requires_host_ingress`, `nonce_source_missing`, `session_turn_completion_gate`, `turn_missing_model_evidence`, `request_class_invalid`, `review_*`, `ingress_nonce_invalid`, `mint_collision_exhausted`, `session_not_open`) alongside QB-AGENT-001's broad `CoreRuntimeError` category, each with an explicit retryable flag: only transport outage and degraded persistence are retryable, with bounded retries (one scoped exception, ACKed by Codex at 2026-10-10T00:12:18Z: the trusted mint adapter, for the exact mint operation only, classifies an authenticated bound mint-endpoint HTTP 404 with no detail code, or a structured `unknown_method` or `method_not_found` result for the expected mint verb, as transport outage and schedules it only for a later failsafe drain, never a hot retry; the generic classifier and the audit outbox in-call retry loop are unchanged, prose, malformed metadata, arbitrary 404s, and unrelated operations never qualify, a 404 with `session_not_open` is final, and every other unknown code stays final); every listed code is final; an unknown code is final, never implies success, and is never retried without limit.
- C10 Review scores travel as their original text: lexical score text is preserved before any numeric coercion through parser, shim, transport, cache, and outbox; Node never converts scores to JS numbers (the D12 raw-token parser reads them as exact strings on Node 18 and later, and exponent and signed forms are rejected); any event hash is computed over the original text, never over a re-serialized parsed value.
- C11 Paginated turn listing and detail reads return `requestClass`, `agentReview`, and `hostileReview`, sanitized as FR-MCP-SESSIONLOGSAN-001 requires, without weakening stable-snapshot pagination, ordered detail retrieval, or browser credential exclusion; this read contract is fixed now, and S4 and S5 implement it on current `develop`.
- C12 The REPL release that includes `workflow.sessionlog.mintTurn`, `workflow.sessionlog.recordReview`, and the lossless score normalizer (S6) comes first; dependent host pins and QB-AGENT-001's P5 artifacts then update against that release; current REPL ownership and pinning work stays separate from advancing the pin. Amendment (ACKed by Codex at 2026-10-10T04:45:14Z in `MEMORY-PROCESS-007`): no accepted QB-AGENT-001 REPL pin may predate the Phase A release providing `workflow.sessionlog.mintTurn`, and the S6 capability remains required wherever it is consumed (QB-AGENT-001's own plan already waits for S6 before accepting a new pin).

## BDPv4 process contract (applies to every slice)

Sources: `docs/Development-Process-draft-v4.md:76` (tests validated with mocks before real code); `docs/Byrd-Dev-Process-v4-Project-Management-Guide.md` 5.1 status machine, 5.2 handoff contract, 5.3 test gate, line 221 loop "Red -> mocks-green -> real Green -> refactor -> full unit gate -> Validating"; `skills/byrd-tdd-process/SKILL.md`.

Requirements DB is authoritative; `docs/Project/*.md` is a generated projection (regenerated, never hand-edited). This overrides the SKILL step 1 wording ("append entries to docs/Project").

Per slice, in order:
1. Contract first: interfaces, DTO members, and skeletons returning neutral values (empty/null/false, never throwing) so RED fails on an assertion. Compile-failure RED only for the first stub of a phase. PowerShell stubs return `$null`/`$false`/`@()`.
2. RED: write the named tests from the ACs; run; record command, `Failed: N, Passed: M, Skipped: 0`, and the first assertion message as a TODO TestDefined checkpoint.
3. MOCKS-GREEN: make the same tests pass against mocks only, no production logic. Pure seams use the CT pattern: abstract `XxxContractTests` with `_Oracle` (hand-written double), `_Production`, and permanent `XxxMutantDetectionTests` (a deliberately wrong double must fail every assertion). Glue slices substitute the seams. Negative check every slice: a wrong mock value makes the target test fail (recorded). Tooling: NSubstitute (Support.Mcp.Tests, Repl.Core.Tests, Support.Mcp.IntegrationTests); hand-written doubles in Client.Tests (`MockHttpHandler`) and McpAgent.Tests; Pester `Mock` plus seams `MCP_PLUGIN_REPL_LOG`/`MCP_PLUGIN_REPL_RESPONSE` (`plugin-hook.ps1:216-227`) and `MCP_PLUGIN_PERSIST_LOG` (`repl-invoke.ps1:1624-1640`).
4. Agent self-review of RED and mocks-green (recorded on the slice's session turn as decisions; from the first deploy whose server stores review records, the Phase B live-check deploy, also as `agentReview`) before any production change. No hostile review before the slice is all green (D10).
5. REAL GREEN: production code; state which tests stay contract tests.
6. REFACTOR: delete `_Oracle` subclasses, keep mutant tests; clean production code.
7. Gates G1-G6 (zero failures, zero skips):
   - G1 targeted `dotnet test <proj>.csproj -c Debug --filter "<filters>"` and/or targeted Pester.
   - G2 `./build.ps1 Test` (it excludes `*IntegrationTests`, `*PluginIntegration*`, `*.Validation`, `*Review.Tests`, and `Build.Tests`, `build/Build.Test.cs:18-24`; slices that change `build/` code also run `dotnet test tests/Build.Tests/Build.Tests.csproj -c Debug`).
   - G3 after G2 green, the integration set the slice names: `tests/QBrainAi.Support.Mcp.IntegrationTests`, `tests/QBrainAi.SessionLog.Validation`, `./build.ps1 MigrationIntegrationTests --skip InstallTestDependencies` (against the PostgreSQL and SQL Server endpoints P0.1 provisions through `MCP_TEST_POSTGRES_CONNECTION` and `MCP_TEST_SQLSERVER_CONNECTION`; `InstallTestDependencies`, on which the target depends, is Windows-only, `build/Build.TestDependencies.cs:45-65`), `./build.ps1 PluginSessionLogIntegration` (from A9 on, after the branch REPL install that the rollout step describes), `tests/QBrainAi.Repl.IntegrationTests`, `tests/QBrainAi.Acid.IntegrationTests`.
   - G4 plugin core: `pwsh -NoProfile -NonInteractive -Command "Invoke-Pester -Path 'plugins/core/test-fixtures/pester/PluginPowerShellRuntime.Tests.ps1','plugins/core/test-fixtures/pester/<touched>.Tests.ps1' -CI"`.
   - G5 `./build.ps1 ValidateConfig` exits 0; `./build.ps1 ValidateTraceability` exits 0 (it fails on any FR missing from the mapping, `build/Build.ValidateTraceability.cs:69-74`, so every catalog FR must be mapped). Phase exits (P0, A9, S11) add `./build.ps1 ValidateTraceability --strict-tr-and-test-coverage` and the scoped use-case gate (operator D3, 2026-10-09: the use-case gate checks only this plan's requirements). Pass rule: (a) the strict run's TR/TEST matrix findings contain no catalog id and no finding absent from the P0 baseline list (P0 records the baseline findings before creating anything; the strict run may exit non-zero only because of baseline findings, and the receipt lists them); (b) `client.UseCases.GetCoverageAsync` lists none of the catalog FRs (the 21 new FRs and the updated FRs in "Updated existing requirements") in `functionalRequirementsWithoutRealizesUseCase`. The global `--strict-use-case-fr-coverage` flag is not a gate: it covers every FR in the store, and `ValidateTraceability` reads use-case links only from a SQLite path (`UseCaseFrTraceabilityLoader.ResolveDefaultSqlitePath`), so the store-backed `GetCoverageAsync` check is authoritative.
   - G6 per-plugin gates (matrix above).
8. Agent self-review of the finished slice (slice turn decisions; from the Phase B live-check deploy also `agentReview`); the slice TODO moves to `Validating`; then the slice's Codex HV runs (D10) and the TODO moves to Complete only after that HV returns an HV pass (`OverallVerdict AGREE`, accuracy and completeness >= 98, and every claim PASS).

Hostile validation protocol (operator directives 2026-10-09: first "at completion of major phases", then refined by D10 to the end of every slice once its gates are green, with HV-P0, HV-A9, and HV-S11 also covering their whole phase; reviewer is Codex CLI, model `gpt-6-sol`, reasoning effort `xhigh`, replacing the earlier PR-comment protocol):
- Major phases and their HV gates: HV-P0 (requirements, use cases, TODOs, plan doc), HV-A (Phase A A1-A9 incl. all plugin repos), HV-B (Phase B S1-S11 incl. all plugin repos). Every slice ends with its own HV once G1-G6 are green (D10): HV-A1..HV-A9 and HV-S1..HV-S11, where HV-A9 also covers all of Phase A (= HV-A) and HV-S11 all of Phase B (= HV-B). Turns of a Code or Docs slice that complete before its HV lands (operator confirmed this interpretation of D10 on 2026-10-09: "agree") record `hostileReview` status `Other` with `otherNotes` "pending slice HV <gate>"; when the slice HV lands, its verdict mapped as FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-005-AC002 states (server-derived Pass or Fail from the scores, or `Other` with `otherNotes` naming the failed claims when a DISAGREE has scores that would derive Pass), with both receipt paths is recorded with `recordReview` onto every turn of that slice, each addressed by its original (`sessionId`, `requestId`) pair (FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC002) (reviews stay writable on completed turns), and the slice HV brief lists those turns. This per-turn recording, and the `Other` "pending slice HV" record before it, apply from the first deploy whose server stores review records, the Phase B live-check deploy (S8 ships `recordReview` in the plugins, but the deployed server gains the review fields only with that deploy). Until it, every slice (A1-A9, S1-S10, and S11 before that deploy) records its verdict on the slice's coordinating turn (dialog and designDecisions with the receipt paths) and with `RecordValidationResultAsync`, and S1-S10 move to Complete on their HV pass. After it, and before the HV-S11 reviewer is launched, the S8-S10 slice verdicts are recorded with `recordReview` onto every turn of those slices by original pair (FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC002), the HV-S11 brief lists those turns with S11's own, and S11 moves to Complete only after `client.SessionLog.QueryAsync` shows every turn listed in that brief carrying its slice verdict; an interrupted backfill is re-run (identical writes are no-ops).
- At each HV point (P0's end, and each slice's end once G1-G6 are green), push the worktree branch (and the branch of each plugin repo the slice or phase changed). Write the HV brief: the claims (with commands, paths, test counts, SHAs, session turn ids), the untrusted implementer receipt, the acceptance rule, mandatory surfaces A-D, the add-profile first action, the review-only scope, and the receipt paths.
- Launch the reviewer with `codex exec -m gpt-6-sol -c model_reasoning_effort="xhigh" --sandbox workspace-write -c sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=true -C <worktree> --json -`, brief on stdin. The reviewer is review-only (it writes only under `docs/receipts/hv/` and to its own MCP session-log turn) and returns claim-by-claim PASS/FAIL/UNKNOWN, `OverallVerdict`, accuracy, completeness, findings, and a `=== VERDICT JSON ===` block. The implementing agent writes the brief and launches the run; it never edits the reviewer's output or verdict. Verify the live model and effort from the Codex session rollout (`turn_context` with `model=gpt-6-sol`, `effort=xhigh`); a run on any other model or effort is not an HV.
- Receipts: the runner writes `docs/receipts/hv/<utc>-<gate>.request.jsonl` (a meta object with the launch command, model, effort, head SHA, and brief SHA-256, plus the full brief body; built from PowerShell objects with `ConvertTo-Json -Compress`) and `.response.jsonl` (the reviewer's complete `--json` event stream). The reviewer writes `hostile-validator-<gate>-<utc>.md` and `.json`. All are committed on the branch only after every marker key value and every `apiKey`, `api_key`, or `X-Api-Key` value of 20 or more characters in them is replaced with `[REDACTED:api-key]`, no other byte changing, and a scan against the current markers' keys finds no match (the repository is public, and the reviewer reads the markers). Store the complete verdict in the MCP session log on the turn that launched the HV (the slice's or P0's coordinating turn) (ordered dialog parts that concatenate byte for byte to the markdown receipt, plus designDecisions, the jsonl paths, and the Codex rollout path); the reviewer also stores it on its own turn, through the supported Codex plugin wrapper (manual `workflow.sessionlog.*` verbs when hooks do not mint), and reads it back; this is mandatory for every HV (slice, phase, and P0): an HV whose reviewer turn cannot be created, populated, completed, and read back is incomplete, its slice stays open, and the coordinator's turn never substitutes for it. From the deploy that carries the S10 completion gate (the Phase B live-check deploy and every later one), the reviewer's own turn meets that gate like any other: the HV brief has the reviewer, through the installed 1.120.0 Codex plugin, set its turn's `requestClass` to `Chore` (a review writes receipts, not product code or documentation, so its hostile record becomes `Unnecessary`) and record its `agentReview` as `Other` with `otherNotes` naming its verdict receipt (a review turn's self-review is its own verdict), before it completes the turn. Because codex reports `turn_logging_unsupported` after a refresh of its installed copy (the installed-equals-synced step, Per-plugin changes) changes its hook definition, until the operator trusts that definition and the codex live assertion passes (host binding table), that trust step and the assertion run after each such refresh and before the next HV reviewer is launched; if they cannot pass, the HV is not launched and the operator is shown the blocker. From the Phase B live-check deploy (the per-turn recording start above), also record it with `workflow.sessionlog.recordReview kind: hostile` (with `requestJsonlPath`/`responseJsonlPath`).
- Pass only with `OverallVerdict AGREE` and accuracy >= 98 and completeness >= 98 and every claim PASS (no FAIL or UNKNOWN). A run that ends without a `=== VERDICT JSON ===` block is recorded as aborted (its receipts committed, not an HV) and a new round is launched. On DISAGREE or any other non-pass (including AGREE with a FAIL or UNKNOWN claim): display every FAIL/UNKNOWN to the operator, fix on the branch, push, and launch a new Codex round whose brief re-attacks the prior receipt; the slice stays open (and, at HV-P0, HV-A9, or HV-S11, its phase).
- Phase PRs (McpServer against `develop`; each changed plugin repo against its remote default branch, `main`, or `master` for opencode; cross-linked) cite the HV receipt paths and verdicts. Merge a phase PR only after an HV pass (`OverallVerdict AGREE`, accuracy and completeness >= 98, and every claim PASS), operator approval, and its repo's CI green on the PR head (for a repo without CI, the G6 receipt for that head; the phase HV receipt cites the run URLs). The next phase starts only after its predecessor has an HV pass (`OverallVerdict AGREE`, accuracy and completeness >= 98, and every claim PASS), operator approval, every phase PR merged (McpServer and each changed plugin repo), and the merged heads verified on their target branches (operator phase rule, `MEMORY-PROCESS-006`); P0 likewise completes before Phase A starts.

TODO transitions (plugin todo workflow / `client.Todo.*`): Planned -> TestDesign (slice start) -> TestReady (RED recorded, `SetTestPlanAsync unitTestsDefined: true`) -> Implementing (mocks-green and negative check recorded, agent self-review recorded) -> Validating (real green, refactor, G1-G6 green, agent self-review of the slice; before this move a `TestPassing` checkpoint (or `ImplementationProgress`) carrying the real-green receipt is appended, and at the gate the slice calls `SetTestPlanAsync` with `unitTestsPassing: true` and, for a slice whose gates include G3, `integrationTestsDefined: true` and `integrationTestsPassing: true`, because `TodoExecutionService.cs:1225-1246` requires implementation evidence to leave Implementing and passing unit and defined integration tests, a passing validation, and satisfied ACs to reach Complete) -> Complete (only after the slice's Codex hostile validation (D10) returns an HV pass (`OverallVerdict AGREE`, accuracy and completeness >= 98, and every claim PASS), recorded via `RecordValidationResultAsync` with the HV receipt paths and jsonl paths); slice DISAGREE -> that slice TODO to Blocked with the findings, fix, re-review; a phase-wide DISAGREE at HV-A9 or HV-S11 reopens every Complete slice it names to TestDesign with the findings (Complete -> Blocked is not a permitted transition).

Session logging during execution: every operator request is its own turn, targeted by explicit `requestId` (`client.SessionLog.BeginTurnAsync/PatchTurnAsync/CompleteTurnAsync`) until Phase A ships; decisions go to `designDecisions`; every write verified server-side with `client.SessionLog.QueryAsync`.

## P0: Requirements, use cases, interfaces, TODOs (no product code)

Baseline already established (2026-10-09, operator-directed, turn `req-20261009T020009Z-prompt-fd7e`): requirements missing from the store were imported from develop `docs/Project/*.md` by non-destructive canonical upsert (`client.Requirements.IngestAsync`, upsert-only per `RequirementsController.cs:847-859`): +13 FR, +18 TR, +19 TEST, +13 mappings, 0 deletions; store then FR 352, TR 480, TEST 512 with 0 develop-doc ids missing (an import-time snapshot; a read-only read at 2026-10-09T22:19Z showed FR 353, TR 481, TEST 513, and P0.3 re-queries the live totals). The hosted GitHub wiki (`3f40792`, 2026-09-23) was not imported: it is staler than the store and wiki mode is authoritative (it would have deleted ACKRECOVERY, RUNAS, SERVICEUPDATE, AGENT-PARITY records). Triage filed for the plugin argv limit and the non-atomic ingest.

1. Toolchain gate on the execution host, recorded as a PlanningDecision: `dotnet --list-sdks` must include an SDK accepted by `global.json` (`10.0.201`, `rollForward: latestFeature`, so any 10.0.2xx or later feature band; on 2026-10-09 LAB-OMARCHY had only `10.0.111`, which fails `./build.ps1` with "A compatible .NET SDK was not found"), Pester 5 must be importable by `pwsh` (none installed on 2026-10-09), `bats` must be on PATH (missing on 2026-10-09), `node`/`npm` must satisfy each TypeScript repo's `engines` (node v26.8.2, npm 11.19.1 present), and `dotnet ef` must run at the EF Core version pinned in `Directory.Packages.props` (`Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Design` 10.0.0); on 2026-10-09 `dotnet ef --version` failed and `.config/dotnet-tools.json` pinned only `docfx`. Missing tools are installed only with operator approval and only through PowerShell: the SDK, on this Linux host, by a PowerShell-only download and extract pinned to 10.0.201: `Invoke-WebRequest` of `https://builds.dotnet.microsoft.com/dotnet/Sdk/10.0.201/dotnet-sdk-10.0.201-linux-x64.tar.gz`, a SHA-512 check against the `hash` that `https://builds.dotnet.microsoft.com/dotnet/release-metadata/10.0/releases.json` lists for that file (stop on mismatch), extraction with `tar -xzf` into `$HOME/.dotnet` invoked from PowerShell, `DOTNET_ROOT` and `PATH` set for the session, then `dotnet --list-sdks` showing 10.0.201 (Microsoft documents `dotnet-install.ps1` for Windows and `dotnet-install.sh` for Linux, so neither script is used here); `dotnet-ef` as a local tool pinned in the repo manifest (`dotnet tool install dotnet-ef --version 10.0.0` from the repo root, committed `.config/dotnet-tools.json`, then `dotnet tool restore`), invoked per provider as `dotnet tool run dotnet-ef migrations add <Name> --project src/QBrainAi.Storage.<Provider>Migrations --startup-project src/QBrainAi.Support.Mcp --context McpDbContext` with `MCP_EF_PROVIDER` (`sqlite`, `postgresql`, `sqlserver`) and `MCP_EF_MIGRATIONS_ASSEMBLY=QBrainAi.Storage.<Provider>Migrations` read by `src/QBrainAi.Storage/McpDbContextFactory.cs:26-30` (form documented in `docs/Operations/permanent-local-audit-sqlserver.md:19-27`; `postgresql` is the canonical PostgreSQL name, `src/QBrainAi.Storage/Database/PostgreSqlMcpDatabaseProviderStrategy.cs:15`); `Install-Module Pester -MinimumVersion 5.0 -Scope CurrentUser`; `bats` via the distribution package (installing it with `pacman` needs root, so the operator runs that install and answers its password prompt). P0.1 also records each Node version in {18, 20, 22, latest} runnable through the host's version manager, and provisions, with operator approval, PostgreSQL and SQL Server test endpoints on this Linux host as containers, setting `MCP_TEST_POSTGRES_CONNECTION` and `MCP_TEST_SQLSERVER_CONNECTION` (both honored by the provider tests), because `MigrationIntegrationTests` depends on `InstallTestDependencies` (`build/Build.TestDependencies.cs:45-65`), which provisions LocalDB with an elevated `msiexec` on Windows only (`build/Build.TestDependencies.cs:236-266`); every G3 use of the target therefore runs `./build.ps1 MigrationIntegrationTests --skip InstallTestDependencies`, and P0.1 records one such run against both endpoints.
