2. Read-only spike (P0.2 writes nothing to the repo or the stores beyond its probe receipts, recorded payloads, and its PlanningDecision; the temporary codex observer files of (c) live outside the repo and are deleted at its end), recorded as a PlanningDecision: confirm from the session transcript and server turns how `<task-notification>` and slash commands arrive at UserPromptSubmit and whether each produces a `queue-operation` `enqueue` record; capture a Claude Code transcript excerpt with `queue-operation`/`enqueue` lines as the A3 arrival-bridge fixture (the bridge design does not depend on whether UserPromptSubmit also fires for queued messages, because on bridge hosts the prompt hook neither mints nor claims); and run every probe the host binding table assigns to P0.2, each recorded with its receipt, an outcome of pass, fail, or unavailable (host not installed or not reachable), and the resulting D6/D7 capability disposition (P0.8 writes it into `docs/AGENT-PLUGIN-FEATURE-MATRIX.md` from these receipts): (a) claude-code: whether every user prompt, in each prompt form named in FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC009, produces a transcript `enqueue` record, and whether `dequeue` always takes the head of the queue; (b) claude-cowork: whether Cowork executes plugin hooks, and the same bridge probes as (a) (every prompt form produces an `enqueue` record, `dequeue` always takes the head); Cowork is supported only if all pass, otherwise `turn_logging_unsupported` (D6) with its feature-matrix row; (c) codex, observed in an isolated probe home, because the codex plugin registers no hooks before A9 (agent plugin matrix) and nothing of the operator's own Codex setup (`~/.codex` and the shared app-server daemon that hosts its interactive sessions) may change: P0.2 records the SHA-256 of `~/.codex/config.toml`, then creates `/tmp/qbrain-p02-codex-<utc>/` holding `observer.ps1` (it reads the hook's stdin JSON and appends `{ observedAtUtc, payload }`, built from native objects with `ConvertTo-Json -Compress`, as one line to `observed.jsonl` in that directory, writes nothing to stdout, and exits 0) and `home/config.toml`. `home` is the `CODEX_HOME` of every probe command (codex-cli 0.160.1 reads its config and auth from `CODEX_HOME`); every probe command sets it with `env` from PowerShell, as the deploy step sets `PATH`; its config sets `approval_policy = "never"`, installs no plugin, and sets `enabled = false` (the `[plugins."<name>"]` key of the operator's own `config.toml`, not in the help output) for every bundled plugin whose manifest declares hooks (on 2026-10-10 `browser@openai-bundled`, `chrome@openai-bundled`, and `unified-computer-use@openai-bundled`), so the probe writes nothing to the MCP stores and the observer is the only enabled hook, and registers inline command hooks for SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, PostToolUse, and Stop (no matcher) that run the observer with `pwsh -NoProfile -NonInteractive -File` (Codex hooks documentation, https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/hooks: inline `[hooks]` tables in a config layer; a non-managed hook runs only once its definition is trusted). The operator signs in inside the probe home once (`env CODEX_HOME=<home> codex login`; the agent never handles the credentials). Run 1 (scripted): `env CODEX_HOME=<home> codex exec --dangerously-bypass-hook-trust -s read-only -C /home/sharpninja/github/McpServer --json "Run the shell command printenv CODEX_THREAD_ID and reply with its output only."`, stdout kept; it passes when the observer recorded a UserPromptSubmit line whose payload carries a nonempty `session_id` and `turn_id`, and it records whether the printed `CODEX_THREAD_ID` equals that `session_id` (the `CODEX_THREAD_ID` verb key of the host binding table is selected only when the same equality also holds in run 2's app-server-hosted session, otherwise the live-session rule). Run 2 (the operator at the keyboard) hosts an interactive session on a private `codex app-server` started only for the probe (the program whose `daemon` subcommand manages the local app-server daemon, which hosts interactive sessions by default: `codex --help` says `--no-daemon` runs "without the shared background server"): in one terminal `env CODEX_HOME=<home> codex app-server --listen unix:///tmp/qbrain-p02-codex-<utc>/app.sock`; in a second, `env CODEX_HOME=<home> codex --remote unix:///tmp/qbrain-p02-codex-<utc>/app.sock -s read-only -a never -C /home/sharpninja/github/McpServer`, where the operator trusts the observer hooks with `/hooks` (Codex hooks documentation; that this works over `--remote` is untested, and if the hooks then do not run, run 2 cannot be performed) and sends prompt A `Run the shell command printenv CODEX_THREAD_ID, then the shell command sleep 90, then reply with the printenv output only` (run 2 records whether that output equals A's `session_id`); while A's `sleep 90` runs, one prompt B per way a message reaches a running session, each with its own text (`Reply with exactly: done B1`, `done B2`, and so on) so each observer line is attributable: each way the TUI offers for a message during a running turn (its on-screen hint names them), and, from a third terminal, `env CODEX_HOME=<home> codex queue --remote unix:///tmp/qbrain-p02-codex-<utc>/app.sock --thread <A's session_id from the observer, used as the "Session UUID" that `--thread` takes; if `codex queue` refuses it, that way fails> --message "Reply with exactly: done Bq"` (`codex queue --help`: "Queue a message for an existing session"); then exit. For each way, B's prompt event fires at enqueue when its UserPromptSubmit `observedAtUtc` is earlier than the `observedAtUtc` of the PostToolUse line for A's `sleep 90` (the PostToolUse line whose command is `sleep 90`) (B was sent while that command ran, so no model step could have taken B in yet), and only when processed otherwise; a B whose prompt event fired at enqueue but that runs as its own later turn (a Stop line carrying B's `turn_id` after A's Stop) also counts as only when processed (queued-message arrival rule, host binding table); a way passes only when its B produced exactly one UserPromptSubmit line (a second line with another `turn_id` would mint a second turn), whose `turn_id` differs from A's and from every other B's, because codex's nonce is `turn_id` (host binding table) and a shared `turn_id` would merge B into A's turn (when a way fires only when processed, P0.2 also records whether the transcript at the payload's `transcript_path` records the enqueue, with its observed payload, for the operator). The codex row fails or is unavailable when run 1 records no UserPromptSubmit line or a payload without `session_id` or `turn_id`, when a way's B, the `codex queue` one included, produces no UserPromptSubmit line, more than one, or one that shares a `turn_id`, or when run 2 cannot be performed; a failed or unavailable codex row, or a result of only when processed for any way, stops P0 and the operator is shown the receipt, because every HV reviewer must persist its own turn through the Codex plugin (host binding table, codex), so every codex arrival path ends either verified or decided by the operator. Afterwards P0.2 copies `observed.jsonl` and run 1's stdout, redacted by the receipt rule of the hostile validation protocol, to `docs/receipts/p0/codex-hook-observer-<utc>.jsonl` and `docs/receipts/p0/codex-hook-probe-exec-<utc>.jsonl`, stops the private app server, signs the probe home out (`env CODEX_HOME=<home> codex logout`), deletes `/tmp/qbrain-p02-codex-<utc>/` (the directory it created), and confirms that `~/.codex/config.toml` still has the recorded SHA-256 and that no file under `~/.codex` contains the probe directory's path (otherwise it stops and shows the operator what changed), so the probe leaves no Codex setting, credential, or path-bearing record in the operator's Codex home; the UserPromptSubmit lines of `codex-hook-observer-<utc>.jsonl` are the recorded codex event payloads that A3's per-host mint cases, A4's mid-turn case, TEST-MCP-PLUGIN-013-AC001, and A9's codex hook cases replay, and that receipt is codex's feature-matrix receipt (P0.8, A9); these runs spawn no subagent, so codex's subagent marker is not observed, and P0.8 records it as unverified in codex's feature-matrix row, the limitation FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-002-AC001 provides for; (d) grok: whether `GROK_SESSION_ID` is set in shell commands, and the same enqueue-versus-processing check for UserPromptSubmit; (e) grok-bot: the Grok Bot cloud runtime's hook payload fields, session environment for shell commands, and queued-message behavior; (f) copilot: two real deliveries of one prompt from two registered hooks and whether their `timestamp` values match (evidence only, D11), two identical prompts sent back to back and one sent while a turn runs with whether their `timestamp` values differ, and whether one message ever reaches the hook twice (a host that gives separate messages equal fields or redelivers in normal use is recorded as a D7 limitation, since its sessions fail closed at the first collision), and the same enqueue-versus-processing check for UserPromptSubmit; (g) cline (classic): whether the installed Cline build runs native hooks, where hook scripts are installed, the UserPromptSubmit payload fields, whether a queued message fires the hook at arrival, and the same equal-field timestamp and redelivery probe as copilot; (h) cline-v2: whether a queued message starts its own run at arrival, and if not, whether a message-queue event exists; (i) opencode: whether `chat.message` fires when a message is created while the session is busy, or only when it is processed. For (d)-(i), the enqueue-versus-processing check records the event's time against the running turn's progress and whether the message then runs inside the running turn or as its own later turn, read from the order of the host's stop or stop-equivalent events (the `turn_id` criterion of (c) is codex's alone). For every host other than the claude-code/claude-cowork bridge and codex (for codex that result stops P0 for the operator), an enqueue-versus-processing result of "only when processed" (which includes a prompt event at enqueue for a message the host runs as its own later turn, queued-message arrival rule) selects D6 `turn_logging_unsupported` with zero mints and writes in this plan, with or without a recorded enqueue event; when one is recorded, P0.2 records that event's observed payload, and P0.8 files a traced follow-up TODO proposing the host's adapter (payload, nonce, correlation, lifecycle, replay, tests) for operator approval; until that adapter is specified, approved, and delivered, the host stays `turn_logging_unsupported` in this plan and its `<Host>_P0_2NotPassed_TurnLoggingUnsupported_ZeroMintsZeroWrites` case applies (operator D5 requires minting at enqueue; FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC006), and each host's adoption tests (A8, A9) include its recorded case. Each check selects between the outcomes the table already defines; no new design is made, and any host limitation it confirms is shown to the operator.

## Requirements catalog

AC ids use `<REQID>-AC###`, the format of the SESSIONLIFE family this plan updates (e.g. `FR-MCP-SESSIONLIFE-001-AC004`). New ACs on existing requirements keep that requirement's existing AC format (`ac-N` for FR-SUPPORT-*, SESSIONLOGSAN, TRIAGESTORE, PLUGININT); requirements that today carry only inline prose ACs get structured `<REQID>-AC###` ACs. In the allocation lists, shorthand `SESSIONTURN-004-001` means `FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-004-AC001`. The bracket after each AC names the slice whose RED test first covers it; "+" lists later slices that extend coverage on another surface. Each AC is verified by the TEST listed under its TR. Each new TEST record carries exactly the three ACs of the rule under "New testing requirements".

### New functional requirements (21)

FR-SUPPORT-016 Turn replace and section API (created in P0. The behavior is cited in source and tests under the legacy id `FR-SUPPORT-010G`, which the requirements grammar rejects (`src/QBrainAi.Repl.Core/RequirementsWorkflow.cs:32-34,400-406`: the suffix must be three digits), so P0 creates it as FR-SUPPORT-016 with TR-SUPPORT-CORE-016 and TEST-SUPPORT-024 (all three verified free in the live store), and S4, the first slice that edits these files, renames every legacy citation; the legacy id is cited 36 times in 9 files per `rg -n -F FR-SUPPORT-010G src tests`: `src/QBrainAi.Services/Services/SessionLogService.cs` (5, including `TurnSection`/`ParseSection` at 1287-1317), `ISessionLogService.cs` (6), `src/QBrainAi.Support.Mcp/Controllers/SessionLogController.cs` (7), `src/QBrainAi.Support.Mcp/McpStdio/FwhMcpTools.SessionLog.cs` (6), `src/QBrainAi.Client/SessionLogClient.cs` (7), `src/QBrainAi.Client/Models/SessionLogModels.cs` (1), `tests/QBrainAi.Support.Mcp.Tests/Services/SessionLogServiceReplaceDeleteTests.cs` (2), and the integration tests `SessionLogControllerTests.cs` (1) and `SessionLogReplaceDeleteControllerTests.cs` (1); also cited by the retired `CODEX-HANDOFF.md:206`, readable with `git show f56dcf70:CODEX-HANDOFF.md`). Statement: the session-log API SHALL support replacing a turn (PUT turn), replacing or resetting one section of a turn (PUT/DELETE section), and deleting one item of a collection section (DELETE item), for the sections actions, tags, context, dialog, commits, designDecisions, requirementsDiscovered, filesModified, blockers, and (after Phase B) agentReview and hostileReview. Same family and AC style as FR-SUPPORT-010E/010F and FR-SUPPORT-014/015 (`ac-N`).
- `ac-1` Replacing a turn clears omitted scalars and collections and preserves the workspace stamp on children. [existing behavior; verified in P0 by `SessionLogServiceReplaceDeleteTests.ReplaceTurnAsync_ClearsOmittedScalarsAndCollections`, `ReplaceTurnAsync_PreservesWorkspaceStampOnChildren`]
- `ac-2` Replacing a section changes only that section; an empty payload clears it; clearing a section removes all its items; additive upsert never removes items. [existing; `ReplaceTurnSectionAsync_ReplacesOnlyNamedSection`, `ReplaceTurnSectionAsync_EmptyPayload_ClearsSection`, `ClearTurnSectionAsync_RemovesAllItemsInSection`, `UpsertTurnAsync_StaysAdditive_DoesNotRemoveExistingItems`]
- `ac-3` Item delete removes exactly the addressed item (string sections by value, commits by SHA, actions by order) and returns false for an unknown key. [existing; `DeleteTurnItemAsync_StringSection_RemovesByValue`, `DeleteTurnItemAsync_Commit_RemovesBySha`, `DeleteTurnItemAsync_Action_RemovesByOrder`, `DeleteTurnItemAsync_UnknownKey_ReturnsFalse`]
- `ac-4` Replacing an unknown section throws `ArgumentException`. [existing; `ReplaceTurnSectionAsync_UnknownSection_Throws`, which asserts exactly this; the alias set in `ParseSection` (`SessionLogService.cs:1300-1317`) is existing behavior but no test asserts it today, so it is not claimed as an AC]
- `ac-5` Sections `agentReview` and `hostileReview` (accepted case-insensitively as `agentreview`, `agent-review`, `agent_review`, `hostilereview`, `hostile-review`, `hostile_review`): replace sets the record (latest wins), clear resets it to the default status except on a completed or failed turn, where it is refused with 400 `review_section_reset_on_terminal_turn` and changes nothing (FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC004), item delete is rejected with 400. [S4 service, S5 REST/MCP/client]

FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001 One turn per user request, with a server-issued request token. Every user message that reaches a host arrival mechanism (prompt hook, arrival bridge, or host-native hook adapter; D5, D6) maps to exactly one session-log turn whose `requestId` the server mints at ingress (operator D1).
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC001 The plugin opens every turn through the server mint operation (`POST /qbrainai/sessionlog/{agent}/{sessionId}/mint` with an `ingressNonce`, or its client and REPL equivalents, called only by hooks and adapters); the server mints `req-<server UTC yyyyMMddTHHmmssZ>-<8 lowercase hex random>`, opens the turn `in_progress`, and returns the token; the plugin never invents a requestId. A minted requestId is unique within the workspace, enforced by the database for minted turns across all sessions; a violation of that requestId index makes the server generate a new requestId (at most 5 retries after the original attempt, 6 attempts in all, then 500 `mint_collision_exhausted`) and never merges into another turn, while a violation of the nonce index means the same event and returns the existing requestId (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC002) (`MinterCollision_ReturnsDistinctId`, `MintCollision_ConcurrentCrossSession_DistinctIds` on SQLite, PostgreSQL, and SQL Server).  [A1 server, A3 plugin +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC002 Repeating the mint with the same agent, MCP session id, and `ingressNonce` (network retry, two hook processes started for one event, or failsafe replay after an outage) returns the same requestId and opens no second turn (server behavior on every host; on copilot and classic cline the hook applies the equal-field rule to a `merged` result of a new invocation, so it fails the host session closed instead of binding, while a stored mint retry or failsafe drain binds normally). A same-nonce mint for a turn that is already terminal returns `merged` with that `storedStatus`, leaves the status unchanged, and the hook neither makes it the write target nor adds it to the open list or the stop ledger (`Mint_SameNonceTerminalTurn_MergedNotReopened` server side; `PromptHook_SameNonceTerminalTurn_Merged_NotTargetNotOpenNotLedger` plugin side). [A1 server, A3 plugin, A4 plugin write target, open list, and ledger +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC003 Two user messages with identical prompt text (an intentional repeat, at any interval; on copilot and classic cline the equal-field rule governs a repeat that also shares the timestamp) are two events, derive two nonces, and receive two requestIds and two turns; two independent hook processes receiving one event (a doubly registered hook) derive one nonce from the host binding table and receive one requestId on every host whose nonce is a stable per-message id (codex, grok, grok-bot, cline-v2, opencode; on bridge hosts the single bridge per host session mints, TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001 AC-006); on copilot and classic cline a second registration is refused before any mint (D11 and the classic-cline single-registration check), so the positive two-process case does not apply there. On copilot and classic cline, whose nonce hashes event fields with no per-message id, two messages whose timestamps differ mint two turns, a second new invocation with equal session, timestamp, and prompt cannot be told apart from a separate message, so it mints nothing and the host session fails closed under D6 (`indistinguishable_arrival`) rather than merging or guessing, and a retry or failsafe replay of one stored mint reuses its nonce (`Copilot_SamePromptDifferentTimestamp_TwoMints`, `Copilot_EqualFieldsSecondInvocation_FailsClosed_NoSecondMint`, `Copilot_MintRetryAndFailsafeReplay_ReuseNonce_NoFailClose`, `Cline_SamePromptDifferentTimestamp_TwoMints`, `Cline_EqualFieldsSecondInvocation_FailsClosed_NoSecondMint`, `Cline_MintRetryAndFailsafeReplay_ReuseNonce_NoFailClose`). [A1 server, A3 plugin, A8 Node hosts +A4 +A9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC004 Distinct prompts open distinct turns. [A3 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC005 A message delivered while another turn is in progress (queued or mid-turn) gets its own turn, minted by the host's arrival mechanism (arrival bridge, prompt hook, or host-native hook adapter); the running turn is not changed. [A4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC006 The Claude hook validator reports `duplicate` when an MCP hook script is registered by both `~/.claude/settings.json` and the enabled plugin, repairs it with a backup, and `-CheckOnly` never mutates settings. [A2]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC007 The hook installer adds no bridge hook for an event the enabled plugin already covers. [A2]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC008 When the server is unreachable at ingress, the hook persists a failsafe mint record carrying its nonce and opens a provisional local turn `pending-<nonce>`; on failsafe drain the server mints idempotently from the nonce and the plugin rebinds the provisional turn and every write bound to it to the minted requestId, in original order. Either id resolves to the same turn; a crash at any point during the rebind loses no write and creates no second turn; a committed pending-to-minted alias stays discoverable and is reapplied until every consumer of it (including an audit outbox) durably acknowledges it, and duplicate delivery or application is harmless (C2). With two or more consumers, an alias acknowledged by one stays discoverable to the others; replay to a consumer stops only after that consumer durably acknowledges, and stops entirely once all have. No server turn ever has a `pending-` requestId: a write captured under a pending id and sent after the alias is sent under the minted id, and the writes of one turn reach the server in their original order even when a close of that turn was made while earlier writes waited in the failsafe. A mint that the server or REPL does not support is recognized only by the trusted mint adapter (PowerShell A3, Node A8) for the exact mint operation, from either an authenticated, bound mint-endpoint response with HTTP 404 and no detail code, or the structured `unknown_method` (GenericClientPassthrough) or `method_not_found` (ReplCommandDispatcher) result for the expected mint verb, as on a server older than A1; it is never inferred from prose, malformed detail metadata, arbitrary 404s, or unrelated operations, keeps the original structured cause, and is classified as a transport outage by that adapter only (never by the generic C9 classifier or the audit outbox in-call retry loop) and handled like one (failsafe retained, turn stays `pending-<nonce>`, retried at a later drain); a mint refused for a final reason (404 with detail code `session_not_open`, 400 validation) reports `turn-open-failed` with the server code, keeps its failsafe record marked final and not retried, invents no requestId, and the stop gate warns naming the prompt. On drain the plugin first replays an idempotent session open for the record's envelope `mcpSessionId` (an outage that spanned SessionStart left that session unopened on the server), then the mint; a `session_not_open` refusal is final only after that session open is confirmed (`OutageSpanningSessionStart_DrainOpensSessionThenMints_NotFinal`). The pending-to-minted lookup entry is permanent for the life of the plugin cache: retiring an alias's consumer-delivery obligation after the last required acknowledgement never removes it, so a reader holding the pending id resolves the minted turn after every acknowledgement and after a restart. After the delivery obligation is retired, `getAlias` still returns the full agreed record with the exact final acknowledgement set, and a repeated acknowledgement is an idempotent no-op. [A3 +A5 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC009 Arrival bridge (D5): on claude-code, and on claude-cowork when P0.2 shows Cowork runs plugin hooks and passes the same bridge probes as claude-code (otherwise FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC004 applies: Cowork reports `turn_logging_unsupported` and the feature matrix records the limitation), the arrival bridge is the only minter: every user message is minted from its transcript `enqueue` record (nonce `q-` from that record) before the model processes it, in state `queued`, and the prompt hook neither mints nor claims, so no hook-to-bridge correlation exists to fail (Claude Code's `queue-operation` records carry no parent `promptId`; the plan never correlates across record kinds without a shared key). One message yields exactly one mint for every prompt form P0.2 captures (plain, slash command, attachment or image, image-only, multi-line CRLF, non-ASCII, at least 1 MiB), and two messages with equal text yield two mints. A pid file naming a dead or foreign process does not prevent a bridge start; a host session ended by the idle rule (`ended.yaml` with `reason: idle`) is live again at its next mint or keyed verb from that host session key, which removes the marker in the same atomic replace under `turn-open.lock` before acting; a keyed verb that revives a session with no delivered request is then refused with `request_unknown` (no write target); a `reason: session_end` marker never revives (`IdleEnded_KeyedVerb_RevivesAtomically_NoTargetRequestUnknown`, `IdleEnded_Revival_CrashRestart_Consistent`, `SessionEndMarker_NeverRevives`); a resumed or forked transcript mints nothing for queue records written before that host session's SessionStart; a transcript shorter than `appliedThroughOffset` is reported with `arrival_bridge_format_unrecognized`, changes no queue entry, records `unsupportedSince` with that reason after a delivered request (unsupported-session rule), and the host session reports `turn_logging_unsupported`, with writes for its already open turns going to the plugin failsafe, until the bridge restarts on a valid transcript (fail closed, like an unknown record shape); the bridge restarts from SessionStart after `/clear` and resume and stops at SessionEnd. If P0.2 shows a user prompt that produces no `enqueue` record, or a `dequeue` that does not take the head, the host is `turn_logging_unsupported` (D6) (`ClaudeCode_P0_2NotPassed_TurnLoggingUnsupported_ZeroMintsZeroWrites`; the real prompt-form fixture case runs only when those probes pass). [A3 +A4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC010 Queue lifecycle: a `queued` request becomes `delivered` (and the host session's write target) at `dequeue` (the head of the queue) or at `remove` with reason `absorbed_mid_turn`, and becomes `withdrawn` at a `popAll` or a `remove` with any other or no reason; minting never moves the write target; a withdrawn request stays `in_progress` on the server until the model closes it (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC009). A record that cannot be attributed to exactly one queued entry (equal content with no shared key: a `remove` matching two or more entries, or a `popAll` carrying fewer lines of a content than there are matching entries) is never guessed: the host session fails closed under D6 (the bridge stops before the record, `unsupportedSince` is recorded, every session-log verb in that host session reports `turn_logging_unsupported` and writes nothing to the server until it ends, writes for its already open turns go to the plugin failsafe and drain at the next SessionStart (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001), and its open turns are listed with their pairs at the next SessionStart); a `popAll` whose line count equals the matching entry count withdraws them all. Every `enqueue` takes a queue position: system events as unminted `kind: system` entries, so a `dequeue` whose head is a system entry delivers nothing and the user request behind it stays `queued` until its own delivery (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-002-AC001). [A3 +A4]

FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-002 Background and system events never open turns.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-002-AC001 Prompts that are `<task-notification>`, `<local-command-*>`, `<agent-message ...>` subagent hand-backs, or only system-reminder blocks, payloads marking a subagent (Claude Code `agent_id`, Grok `subagentType`), and deliveries with `MCP_SUBAGENT_ID`/`GROK_SUBAGENT_ID` set open no turn, call no mint, leave turn state unchanged, and report `system-event-skipped`; classification uses these structural markers only, never the topic or wording of the prompt. On every supported host a prompt event produced by a subagent or a child session (for example an opencode `task` child session's `chat.message`) opens no turn; P0.2 records each host's subagent marker, and a host without one records the limitation in the feature matrix. On bridge hosts a system-event `enqueue` still occupies a queue position (`kind: system`), so its later `dequeue` or removal never delivers or withdraws a user request (`Bridge_SystemEnqueueAheadOfUser_SystemDequeue_UserStaysQueued`, `Bridge_SystemEnqueue_Removed_NoTurn`, `Bridge_SystemAndUserInterleaved_CrashReplay_ZeroWrongTurnWrites`; the `MCP_SUBAGENT_ID`/`GROK_SUBAGENT_ID` deliveries `SystemEvents_SubagentEnvMarkers_NoMint`; the opencode child session `opencodeChildSession_chatMessage_noTurn`). [A3 +A4 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-002-AC002 Slash-command (`<command-name>`) and `<user_query>` prompts open turns, and so does any user prompt that happens to contain a hostile-review or delegation brief (for example the brief that launches a Codex hostile reviewer, which needs its own reviewer turn). [A3 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-002-AC003 Hooks of subagents or parallel sessions sharing the workspace cache never change this session's turn state. [A4 +A8]

FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-003 A new prompt never cancels an in-progress turn.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-003-AC001 Opening a turn never persists `canceled` for any other turn. [A4 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-003-AC002 The prior in-progress turn stays `in_progress` locally and on the server and remains addressable by requestId. [A4 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-003-AC003 Only the model closes turns; no hook calls complete or fail. [A7]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-003-AC004 The stop ledger lists every request delivered since the last passing Stop, plus every earlier entry whose close the server has not confirmed; a request that is minted but still `queued` is not in it until it is delivered (the single enrollment rule of FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001). Only a server-confirmed close leaves the ledger (A5 outcome mapping, `CloseVerb_OnlyServerConfirmedClose_LeavesOpenListAndLedger`), and the Stop gate evaluates that ledger plus one separate scan of `withdrawn` queue entries still `in_progress` (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC009, TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-006 AC-005), so a request withdrawn before delivery is blocked as `turn_withdrawn_unclosed` without ever entering the ledger (A7, `StopGate_LedgerListsDeliveredSinceLastPass_PlusUnconfirmedCloses`, `StopGate_WithdrawnBeforeDelivery_EmptyLedger_BlocksWithdrawnUnclosed_FailByPair_NextStopPasses`). [A4 +A5 +A7]

FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-004 Truthful persist outcomes.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-004-AC001 Session submit returns a per-turn outcome, exactly one of `created`, `merged`, or `skipped_terminal`, with `storedStatus` and `reason` on REST, MCP `sessionlog_submit`, and the typed client; there is no `skipped_duplicate` outcome on any surface (operator, 2026-10-09: "Remove"); a resubmit identical to the stored turn reports `merged` with reason `no_change`; duplicate entries for one requestId in one submission are applied last-wins (today's behavior) and report one outcome for that requestId. [A1]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-004-AC002 The plugin maps `skipped_terminal` to `persisted:false`, code `skipped_terminal`, non-zero exit, and a cleared failsafe record. [A5 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-004-AC003 Omitted-status enrichment and review-section writes on terminal turns report `merged` or success (review-section writes from S4, `SessionLogTurnReviewSectionTests.SectionPut_TerminalTurn_ReportsSuccess`). [A1 +S4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-004-AC004 A plugin verb targeting a locally terminal turn is refused with `turn_immutable` without a server call, except `recordReview` and requestClass-only updates (the exception from S8 and S9, when those verbs ship: `TerminalTurn_RecordReviewAndClassOnly_Accepted_OtherVerbsTurnImmutable`, `terminalTurn_recordReviewAndClassOnly_accepted_otherVerbsTurnImmutable`). [A5 +A8 +S8 +S9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-004-AC005 A result from a server without per-turn outcomes is reported `outcomeVerified:false`. [A5 +A8]

FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005 The stop gate enforces and never closes turns.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001 Blocks with `turn_in_progress` when a delivered request in the stop ledger (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-003-AC004) is still in progress; a withdrawn request still in progress blocks under AC009, and a minted request still `queued` only warns. A request in state `queued` (minted, not yet delivered) never produces `turn_in_progress`; the gate warns, naming its requestId and enqueue time. A Stop in one host session never blocks or warns on another host session's turns (`StopGate_OtherHostSession_NeverBlocksOrWarns`). A request enters the stop ledger when it is delivered, so a request minted before a passing Stop and delivered after it is evaluated at the next Stop (`StopGate_EnqueueR2_PassForR1_DequeueR2_NextStopBlocksUntilClosed`, including a restart between the two Stops). Unsupported-session failsafe: in a host session with `unsupportedSince` (whatever its reason, host binding table unsupported-session rule), every session-log write the model makes for a turn of that host session still in progress when `unsupportedSince` was recorded (a delivered open request, or a `withdrawn` queue entry still `in_progress`, addressed by its original pair; a minted request still `queued` is not covered and stays a warning) (evidence actions, design decisions, `requestClass`, and the close call; a review is never captured, keeping the no-failsafe rule of TR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-005 AC-002 and S9, and is refused with `turn_logging_unsupported`) is captured as a plugin failsafe record that carries the turn's C4 binding and root-id envelope, is marked `unsupported_session`, and is never sent to the server during that host session; every mint and every write for any other turn stays refused with `turn_logging_unsupported` and captures nothing (`UnsupportedSession_MintOrWriteForOtherTurn_Refused_NothingCaptured`). The Stop gate evaluates such a turn with its captured records counted: it blocks until the model's close is captured, with a remedy that names only the missing model evidence, the missing `requestClass` (from S8/S9, when the plugin's requestClass verb ships; a class write is captured like any other write), and the close call (all accepted, AC002; the plugin pre-check lets that close through, FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-004-AC002), because only the completion gate's terminal reviews cannot be written in that session; it then only warns with the cause and the retained failsafe records. The completion gate is judged at the drain: the server's gate judges the drained close, and a gate rejection keeps the turn in progress and listed with its original pair at that SessionStart, where its reviews are recorded through the C5 pair (FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC002) before it is closed (`UnsupportedSession_ReviewRefused_NotCaptured`, `UnsupportedSession_CodeTurn_DrainedCloseRejectedByGate_StaysListed_ReviewViaPair_ThenCloses`). The next SessionStart for the agent drains the captured records in original order through the trusted recovery flow with the turn's original (MCP `sessionId`, `requestId`) pair (C5); a record whose turn is no longer in progress on the server is retained and warned, never forced. Each reason is covered from an earlier open turn (`StopGate_UnknownBridgeRecord_EarlierOpenTurn_CloseCaptured_ThenWarnsOnly`, `StopGate_TruncatedTranscript_EarlierOpenTurn_CloseCaptured_ThenWarnsOnly`, `StopGate_SyntheticUnsupportedSince_AnyReason_EarlierOpenTurn_CloseCaptured_ThenWarnsOnly` in A7; the real duplicate-hook transition `DuplicateHookAfterDelivery_UnsupportedSince_OpenTurnCloseCaptured` for copilot in A9 and `nodeDuplicateHookAfterDelivery_unsupportedSince_openTurnCloseCaptured` for classic cline in A8). A withdrawn-before-delivery turn is covered too: its decision, its `requestClass` (from S8/S9), and `failTurn` by original pair are captured, the next Stop warns instead of blocking, and the drain closes it (after S10 the server gate accepts that fail, which needs only the class) (`StopGate_WithdrawnBeforeDelivery_ThenUnsupported_CloseCapturedByPair_NextStopWarns_DrainCloses`). A Stop in another live host session still blocks on that session's own turns (`StopGate_EqualFieldFailClosed_EarlierOpenTurn_CloseCapturedToFailsafe_ThenWarnsOnly_DrainsWithOriginalPair`, `StopGate_BridgeAmbiguity_EarlierOpenTurn_CloseCapturedToFailsafe_ThenWarnsOnly_DrainsWithOriginalPair`). Outage close: a model close held in the ordinary plugin failsafe because of a transport outage turns that turn's block into the existing degraded persistence warning (AC005); its ledger entry stays until the server confirms the close (`StopGate_OutageCloseQueued_WarnsDegraded_LedgerKeptUntilConfirmed`). [A7 +A8 +A9 +S8 +S9 +S10]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC002 The plugin close verbs refuse to close a turn that has no model-authored action, design decision, or commit (the evidence predicate TR-MCP-PLUGIN-011-AC003 keeps), with code `turn_missing_model_evidence`; the turn stays in progress, so the stop gate blocks it as `turn_in_progress` with the accumulated remedy of the wire contract (every unmet prerequisite, then the valid close call). A turn closed without model evidence by another surface accepts no plugin edit, so the gate reports it as a warning naming it and never blocks on it. Every stop-gate block names a remedy the plugin accepts on the blocked turn's current status (`StopGate_EveryBlockRemedy_AcceptedOnBlockedTurnStatus`, `CloseVerb_NoEvidence_Refused_TurnStaysInProgress`, `StopGate_ClosedWithoutEvidenceElsewhere_WarnsOnly`, `CloseVerb_CommitOnlyEvidence_Accepted`). [A7 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC003 Blocks with `session_turn_completion_gate` a turn whose most recent close the server refused with that code (recorded locally from the server response; such a turn is still in progress), naming the server's missing items first in its remedy (`StopGate_ServerGateRefusal_BlocksWithServerMissingItems`), except for an already open turn of a host session with `unsupportedSince`, whose completion gate is judged at the drain (AC001). There is no marker condition: the server gate (FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-002) is the only completion authority. Its remedy is the accumulated per-turn remedy of the wire contract, so it also names any missing model evidence. [S8 +S9 +S10]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC004 Never calls `completeTurn` or `failTurn`. [A7 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC005 Warns without blocking for model-canceled turns, retained failsafe records, and degraded persistence. The warning list is exactly: model-canceled turns, `queue_removal_ambiguous`, `indistinguishable_arrival`, and `duplicate_prompt_hook` (each, and `arrival_bridge_format_unrecognized` when it set `unsupportedSince`, or any other `unsupportedSince` reason, naming the open turns of its fail-closed host session and their captured `unsupported_session` failsafe records), queued undelivered requests, retained failsafe records (including final mint refusals and unbound legacy records), degraded persistence, `duplicate_delivery_suspected`, `nonce_source_missing`, `arrival_bridge_format_unrecognized`, `stop_gate_overridden_by_host`, open turns of ended host sessions, a turn closed without model evidence by another surface, a live host session whose arrival bridge is not running, and a turn in progress in the legacy `current-turn.yaml` at upgrade (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC004, `StopGate_LegacyCurrentTurnInProgress_Warned`). [A7]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC006 Passes and clears the ledger only when every check passes. Every Stop invocation re-evaluates the ledger, including when the host reports that an earlier Stop already blocked (Claude Code `stop_hook_active`, Grok `stopHookActive`): while any turn remains open it blocks again with the same findings. If the host ends the session anyway (its own continuation limit), the gate records `stop_gate_overridden_by_host` with the open turn ids in the ledger, and the next SessionStart or prompt hook shows it as a warning until the model closes those turns. A ledger entry whose closing write the server has not confirmed terminal stays in the ledger across passing Stops until the server reports it terminal. Turns still in progress when their host session ends (any reason, including `/clear` and a host override) stay in progress and are listed at the next SessionStart for that agent in the workspace with their original (MCP `sessionId`, `requestId`) pairs; closing one through the trusted recovery flow with that pair succeeds, and a `requestId` alone is refused (C5). [A7 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC007 The existing failed-build block is retained. [A7]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC008 Warns (never merges, never blocks) with `duplicate_delivery_suspected` when one host session minted two requestIds within 2 seconds for prompts whose SHA-256 of the normalized text is equal, naming both requestIds so the operator can check hook registration; the warning is diagnostic only (it can fire only where a host's event identity is weaker than expected) and is not evidence of D1 compliance. [A7]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC009 Blocks with `turn_withdrawn_unclosed` while a `withdrawn` request is still `in_progress`, naming it, its withdrawal record (`popAll` or `remove` reason), and the action: close it with `workflow.sessionlog.failTurn` (`requestId` override, reason "withdrawn before delivery"); hooks still never close it. The instruction text includes a designDecision and, from S8/S9 (when the plugin's requestClass and recordReview verbs ship), the `requestClass` the close needs, so following it exactly makes the next Stop pass (`StopGate_WithdrawnFailedPerInstruction_NextStopPasses`). [A7]

FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-006 Decision lists through workflow verbs.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-006-AC001 `updateTurn`, `appendActions`, `completeTurn`, `failTurn` accept `designDecisions`, `requirementsDiscovered`, `blockers` and persist them. [A6 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-006-AC002 Those lists are part of the dedupe fingerprint, so a change to only them is persisted. [A6]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-006-AC003 Model-evidence counters count only model-invoked verbs, never hook-invoked ones (Node core: `nodeHookAuthoredWrites_notEvidence`). [A6 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-006-AC004 One decision call (`appendDecisions`, or a decision passed to `updateTurn`/`completeTurn`/`failTurn`) writes the workspace triple (C7): a `designDecisions` entry, a dialog item with category `decision`, and a `design_decision` action; replaying the same call adds nothing. [A6 +A8]

FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007 Independent turn edits.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC001 Each turn has its own state file and lock; each host session has one state file `sessions/<hostSessionKey>/turn-state.yaml` holding its write target (the request most recently delivered to the model; D9 refuses unqualified writes while several delivered requests are open), its open delivered requests, its queue (minted requests with `queued`/`delivered`/`withdrawn` state), `appliedThroughOffset`, and the host session's own `mcpSessionId` that its SessionStart records (Node: `turn-state.json`, recorded by the first ingress event for that host session key, because the Node hosts have no SessionStart event), always replaced atomically (temp file, flush, rename) under `turn-open.lock`; `current-turn.yaml` remains only as a read-only legacy status file that no verb uses for targeting. The arrival bridge, the mint, and every verb read the MCP session id only from their own host session's turn-state; the workspace-level `session-state.yaml` is a legacy file never used for targeting, and the rotated-sessionId rebind (`repl-invoke.ps1:1954-1965`, TR-MCP-PLUGIN-012) is never applied to per-turn files, so a second host session's SessionStart never retargets the first (`TwoHostSessions_OwnMcpSessionIds_SecondSessionStartNeverRetargetsFirst`, Node `twoHostSessions_ownMcpSessionIds_secondSessionStartNeverRetargetsFirst`). [A4 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC002 Every verb resolves exactly one target turn, in this precedence: explicit `requestId` override, else the invocation's bound request, which is refused with `request_ambiguous` while two or more delivered requests are open in the host session (D9); it mutates only that turn; an explicit requestId resolves to exactly one immutable owning MCP session within the validated workspace and agent, and naming a turn of an earlier host session requires its original (MCP `sessionId`, `requestId`) pair, used only by recovery and review backfill (C5); an unknown explicit requestId is refused with `request_unknown`, and a terminal one with `turn_immutable` (except the review and class writes allowed on terminal turns, FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-004-AC004). A verb whose host session has no delivered request (no write target) is refused with `request_unknown` naming the host session key and writes nothing (`Binding_NoWriteTarget_RequestUnknown_NamesHostSessionKey`). A `request_ambiguous` refusal lists each open delivered request's requestId, delivery time, source, and title. [A4 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC003 Concurrent edits to two turns from separate processes lose nothing and cross-contaminate nothing. [A4 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC004 Failsafe records and fingerprints are namespaced by requestId; replay never targets another turn. A failsafe record without a captured binding (written before the upgrade) is never replayed automatically: it is retained and listed with its path by the stop gate's retained-failsafe warning; a turn in progress in the legacy `current-turn.yaml` at upgrade is listed as a warning by the first SessionStart or Stop and stays addressable by explicit requestId (`Failsafe_LegacyUnbound_NeverAutoReplayed_Retained`, `LegacyCurrentTurnInProgress_ListedAtFirstSessionStart_AddressableByRequestId` in A4; the Stop warnings `StopGate_UnboundLegacyFailsafe_WarnedWithPath`, `StopGate_LegacyCurrentTurnInProgress_Warned` in A7). [A4 +A7 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC005 Opening, closing, or canceling one turn never changes another turn locally or on the server. [A4 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC006 Host-context binding (operator D2): at the start of each verb invocation the plugin resolves the invoking host session's key by the host's locked method (see the host binding table) and binds the invocation to that host session's write target; the binding is written into the invocation's persist and failsafe records and never re-resolved, so a prompt minted after the invocation started never retargets it. On hosts with an arrival bridge, the invocation records the transcript length when it starts and then applies, under `turn-open.lock`, exactly the transcript records that end at or before that length (records appended after it started are left for later invocations), so the binding is the invocation-start snapshot D2 requires, so its binding reflects every delivery recorded before it began. [A4 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC007 Interleaving: with R1 delivered and R2 enqueued mid-turn in the same host session, a verb started after R2's mint but before R2's delivery (`absorbed_mid_turn` or `dequeue`) writes to R1; a verb started after R2's delivery without `requestId` is refused with `request_ambiguous` while R1 and R2 are both open (D9); with explicit ids an R1, R2, R1 sequence of writes lands on R1, R2, R1 respectively; once R1 is closed an unqualified verb writes to R2; a withdrawn R2 never becomes a write target; R1 stays in progress throughout. When the verb cannot read its host session key, the live-session rule applies: with exactly one live host session of its agent it binds there with warning `host_session_inferred`; with none or several it is refused (`host_session_unknown`, `host_session_ambiguous`) unless it carries the trusted (MCP `sessionId`, `requestId`) pair of C5 that the server verifies for this workspace and agent; a `requestId` alone is refused the same way; with two concurrent host sessions in one workspace and no keys, zero writes land on the wrong turn. After a prompt's open fails (`turn-open-contended`, `turn-open-failed`, `nonce_source_missing`), unqualified verbs in that host session are refused with `request_unknown` until a later delivery succeeds, so that prompt's work never lands on the earlier turn (`Binding_AfterFailedOpen_UnqualifiedVerb_RequestUnknown`). A turn the server reports terminal, whichever surface closed it, stops counting as an open delivered request at the next verb or Stop, and a request leaves the open list only when the server confirms its close (a queued, refused, or failed close leaves it open; A5 outcome mapping `CloseVerb_OnlyServerConfirmedClose_LeavesOpenListAndLedger`, A7 Stop `StopGate_TurnClosedElsewhere_DroppedFromOpenList`). When the write target is closed while an earlier delivered request is still open, an unqualified verb is refused with `turn_immutable` naming the open request (`Binding_WriteTargetClosed_EarlierStillOpen_TurnImmutable`). [A4 +A5 +A7 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC008 Session changes in place never misattribute: after Claude Code `/clear`, a shell verb binds to the new session's write target (its `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` is updated), a long-lived process resolves per call by the live-session rule (the cleared session is ended by SessionEnd reason `clear`), and after resume the resumed session's key is used. [A4 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC009 Every record family the plugins write for a turn (queue entry, alias, failsafe record, stop-ledger entry inside `turn-state.yaml`, persisted or queued event) carries the root-id envelope `{ workspacePath, agent, mcpSessionId, hostSessionKey, ingressNonce, requestId }` (`requestId` may be the pending alias until rebind), while a session-scoped record that belongs to no turn (a `kind: system` queue entry, `unsupportedSince`) carries the session-level envelope `{ workspacePath, agent, mcpSessionId, hostSessionKey }` plus its own key (`enqueueOffset`, `causeOffset`, on a prompt-hook host `causeNonce`, or, for a cause with neither, its `reason` and `detectedAtUtc`), never an invented nonce or requestId, so each record is attributable on its own without consulting another record (operator, 2026-10-09: "The root id of whatever something is a child of or depends on, should always be carried."; design principle: "Always have a traceable relationship when coordinating two disparate entities."); each family is read back after write and after replay with the envelope intact (`RootIds_EveryRecordFamily_PresentAfterWriteAndReplay`, Node `rootIds every record family present after write and replay`, including the alias and an outbox event). [A3 +A4 +A8]

FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008 All agent plugins enforce the turn rules.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC001 The Node core satisfies, for its tools and cache (jest), exactly the ACs in the A8 allocation, including the stop-equivalent rules FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001, -AC002, -AC004, and -AC006 through cline-v2 `afterRun` and opencode `session.idle` (in A8 driven through recorded host payload fixtures; the host repos run them in A9). Every Node session tool keeps the structured failure cause of a response (detail code and retryable flag, through the exported `classifyOperationFailure` / `CoreOperationError`) and never builds a string-only error, so C9 detail codes such as `mint_collision_exhausted` reach the caller (`sessionTool_preservesStructuredCause_C9`); a governed context uses only its bound bridge and never an ambient HTTP fallback such as `session-shim.ts` `querySessionHistoryHttpFallback`, and a missing bridge is a typed final failure (`governedContext_noAmbientHttpFallback`). A governed flush (`host-context.flushCacheBestEffort`, and the legacy cache use in `session-shim`) uses only the context-bound durable outbox path, keeps its pending, rejected, and quarantined completion blockers, and never replays a global or foreign-workspace failsafe directory through a governed bridge; suppressing the legacy flush is not audit clearance (`governedFlush_contextBoundOutboxOnly_noForeignReplay`). Both governed cases run in A8 against the port doubles: the outbox port double (of `AuditOutbox.enqueue` and `AuditAliasConsumer.enqueue`, as in the `nodeOutbox_*` cases) and a bound-bridge port double of `src/transport/*`. [A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC002 cline, cline-v2, and opencode adopt the Node core and pass their suites. [A9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC003 claude-code, claude-cowork, codex, copilot, grok, and grok-bot receive the synced PowerShell core and pass their suites. [A9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC004 Every host either has an arrival mechanism listed in the host binding table (operator D6) or reports `turn_logging_unsupported` on every session-log verb and writes nothing; no host relies on the model to open turns. While the copilot or classic-cline duplicate check fails, every session-log verb of that plugin, not only its prompt hook, reports `turn_logging_unsupported` and writes nothing to the server; when the check starts failing in a host session after one of its requests was delivered, the first verb or hook that observes it records `unsupportedSince` with reason `duplicate_prompt_hook`, and writes for a turn already open go to the plugin failsafe (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001, `nodeDuplicateHookAfterDelivery_unsupportedSince_openTurnCloseCaptured` for classic cline in A8, `DuplicateHookAfterDelivery_UnsupportedSince_OpenTurnCloseCaptured` for copilot in A9). [A8 +A9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC005 All nine plugin versions are bumped and reported correctly by the marker `agent_plugins` section after a server restart. The marker generator (`MarkerFileService.BuildDefaultAgentPlugins`, `src/QBrainAi.Services/Services/MarkerFileService.cs:532-611`, five entries today) gains four contracts: `ClaudeCowork` (mcpserver-claude-cowork-plugin), `GrokBot` (mcpserver-grok-bot-plugin), `ClineV2` (mcpserver-cline-v2-plugin), and `OpenCode` (mcpserver-opencode-plugin), each with source type, plugin name, version read from that plugin's manifest, activation, startup command, required environment, hook and tool expectations, and root hints in the same shape as the existing five; P0.7 confirms each plugin's `PLUGIN_AGENT_NAME` and source type from its repository and records any difference as a RequirementRefined decision before A9; a marker read back after restart lists all nine with their bumped versions (`MarkerFileService_AgentPlugins_NineEntries_VersionsFromManifests`). [A9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC006 `docs/AGENT-PLUGIN-FEATURE-MATRIX.md` lists, for each of the nine plugins and each turn-logging feature (ingress mint, arrival bridge or adapter, host-context binding, stop gate), whether it is supported, adapted, or a documented capability limitation (D7) with the reason and the P0.2 receipt; every `turn_logging_unsupported` host appears there as a limitation. A host whose queued messages reach no arrival mechanism at enqueue is `turn_logging_unsupported` (D6; operator D5 requires minting at enqueue) with zero mints and writes until an enqueue adapter exists, and its row states that outcome; a P0.2 probe with outcome `unavailable` selects the row's stated default where one exists, and for an arrival mechanism selects D6 `turn_logging_unsupported` with reason "P0.2 unavailable", shown to the operator. The claude-code and claude-cowork rows also record the D6 limitation that an unattributable queue record (equal-content queued messages with no shared key) fails that host session closed for the rest of the session (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC010). [A9]

FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-001 Request classification.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-001-AC001 `requestClass` accepts `Code`, `Docs`, `Chore` case-insensitively and returns PascalCase. [S1]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-001-AC002 Other values, empty strings, and `Unclassified` from agent surfaces are rejected with `request_class_invalid`. [S1 +S5]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-001-AC003 An omitted value keeps the stored value on additive writes; PUT replace resets it. [S4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-001-AC004 The migration backfills existing rows with `Unclassified`. [S3]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-001-AC005 Turns can be created and updated without a class. [S4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-001-AC006 The field is readable and writable on REST, MCP, client, REPL, and McpAgent. [S5 +S6 +S7]

FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-002 Completion gate.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-002-AC001 `completed` requires `requestClass`, `agentReview` in Pass/Fail/Other, and for Code/Docs `hostileReview` in Pass/Fail/Other; for Chore `hostileReview` is not checked. For the gate `Unclassified` counts as missing. The gate evaluates the turn as it will be stored after the same write's class change, Chore flip, and review records, so one write carrying `requestClass`, terminal reviews, and `completed` succeeds. [S1 policy, S10 enforcement]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-002-AC002 `failed` requires `requestClass` only. [S1, S10]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-002-AC003 The gate applies on every surface: submit, upsert, PATCH, PUT, complete, fail, MCP tools, REPL, McpAgent. [S10]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-002-AC004 It fires only on a transition into `completed`/`failed` or a PUT carrying a terminal status; additive writes to already-terminal turns are not re-gated. A transition into `canceled` is not a completion and is not gated, a deliberate difference from the QBAgent evidence gate (`IsTerminalTurnStatus` counts canceled), so that client-supplied canceled persists (FR-MCP-TRIAGESTORE-001) keep working; it never satisfies the gate, and the stop gate reports a model-canceled turn as a warning. [S10]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-002-AC005 A rejection is atomic and leaves the data context reusable. A submission in which any turn fails the gate is rejected as a whole with 400 `session_turn_completion_gate` naming each failing requestId, and no turn of that submission is persisted. [S10]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-002-AC006 The error is 400 `validation_error` with token `session_turn_completion_gate` and missing items in fixed order (requestClass, agentReview, hostileReview). [S1 message, S5 classifier, S10]

FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-003 Exempt write origins.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-003-AC001 Import, ServerInternal, and Replication origins bypass the gate. [S10]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-003-AC002 ServerInternal writers stamp `Chore`; Import and Replication stamp `Unclassified` when no class arrives; no review is ever fabricated. Import and Replication store a supplied review record exactly as received (status and stamped thresholds, not re-derived) after the same per-status and score validation as Agent writes; an invalid record is rejected with its `review_*` code. [S10]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-003-AC003 No REST, MCP, REPL, client, or plugin code can select a non-Agent origin (architecture test). [S10]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-003-AC004 QBAgent internal tool completions are gated. [S10]

FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-004 Plugin class and gate surfaces.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-004-AC001 Plugin verbs accept `requestClass`. [S8 +S9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-004-AC002 The local pre-check is advisory: before the close is sent it computes the missing completion items with the exact gate message (the golden-contract rules) and reports them as a warning; it never refuses a close. A class or review recorded by another process, host session, or surface before the completion is honored and is not reported missing. The server gate (FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-002, S10) is the only completion authority, so nothing refuses a close that a pre-S10 or rolled-back server would accept, and no activation signal is needed: this plan adds no marker field, and the marker and its signature stay exactly as they are (FR-MCP-140, TR-MCP-SEC-005, TEST-MCP-189 unaffected). When the server refuses a close with 400 `session_turn_completion_gate`, the surface reports the server's message and missing items, the turn stays in progress, and AC003 applies (`Precheck_Advisory_WarnsMissingItems_NeverRefuses`, `nodePrecheck_advisory_warnsMissingItems_neverRefuses`). Unsupported session (operator option 1, FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001): for a turn of that host session still in progress when `unsupportedSince` was recorded (a delivered open request, or a `withdrawn` queue entry still `in_progress`, addressed by its original pair; a minted request still `queued` is not covered and stays a warning), the close goes to the `unsupported_session` failsafe with no server write per FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001 (the advisory warning still prints), and the server gate judges it at the drain; the model-evidence check (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC002) still applies. This is plugin-core state only; the McpAgent pre-check of TR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-004 has no host session (`Precheck_UnsupportedSession_CodeTurnMissingReviews_CloseCaptured_NoServerWrite`, `nodePrecheck_unsupportedSession_codeTurnMissingReviews_closeCaptured`). [S8 +S9 +S10]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-004-AC003 A close that carries actions, decisions, requirements, or blockers is sent as two writes: the carried evidence first, as an additive write that changes no status (so the completion gate never applies to it, FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-002-AC004) and is verified by readback, then the status transition. The failsafe keeps each part until that part is verified on the server, so a crash between them replays only what is missing and replay is idempotent. A server gate rejection of the transition therefore loses no evidence: it clears only the transition part, leaves the turn in progress, and reports the refusal with the server's missing items (`CloseWithEvidence_EvidenceWriteVerifiedBeforeTransition_GateRefusalLosesNothing`, `closeWithEvidence_evidenceWriteVerifiedBeforeTransition_gateRefusalLosesNothing` against a server double in S8/S9; `RealGate_RejectedCloseEvidence_SurvivesRestart_ReadBackEveryField`, `realGate_rejectedCloseEvidence_survivesRestart_readBackEveryField` against the real gate in S10). This includes a drained `unsupported_session` close: its `session_turn_completion_gate` rejection clears that failsafe record and keeps the turn in progress and listed with its original pair. The close's carried writes follow this rule until its reviews are recorded through the C5 pair and a later close is accepted (`UnsupportedSession_CodeTurn_DrainedCloseRejectedByGate_StaysListed_ReviewViaPair_ThenCloses`, `nodeUnsupportedSession_codeTurn_drainRejected_reviewViaPair_thenCloses`, run against the real gate in S10). [S8 +S9 +S10]

FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001 Turn review records.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC001 Each turn exposes `agentReview` and `hostileReview` with fields `status`, `otherNotes`, `correctness`, `completeness`, `correctnessThreshold`, `completenessThreshold`, `reasons`, `reviewer`, `reviewedAt`, `requestJsonlPath`, `responseJsonlPath`. [S2]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC002 The server creates `agentReview=Unstarted` and `hostileReview=Unstarted` at turn creation. [S4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC003 A review write replaces the whole record (latest wins, reasons not merged); an identical write is a no-op. A no-op is a write whose resulting stored record (derived status and stamps included) equals the current one. [S4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC004 An omitted review keeps the stored record; a PUT turn omitting it and a section DELETE reset it to the default status. The default status is the creation default for the turn's current class: `hostileReview` `Unnecessary` when the class is Chore, otherwise `Unstarted`; `agentReview` `Unstarted`. On a completed or failed turn a review section DELETE is refused with 400 `review_section_reset_on_terminal_turn` and changes nothing. [S4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC005 Every read surface returns both records, sanitized (REST, MCP, client, REPL, federation snapshot, transaction restore). [S4 +S5 +S6]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC006 Existing rows are backfilled with `agentReview=Unstarted` and `hostileReview=Unstarted`. [S3]

FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002 Review writes on any turn status.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC001 Section PUT `agentReview`/`hostileReview` works on in-progress, completed, failed, canceled, and legacy turns and changes no other field or status. [S4 +S5]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC002 `recordReview` (MCP tool, client, REPL, plugins) writes only the review; for a turn of an earlier MCP session (review backfill, including the D10 slice verdict) the plugin surfaces accept only that turn's original (MCP `sessionId`, `requestId`) pair, verified against the workspace and agent and captured once, and refuse a `requestId` alone or a pair from another conversation with `request_unknown` (C5). Non-plugin surfaces (MCP tool, REST, client, REPL) refuse a pair that does not exist for the named agent in the workspace with `request_unknown`. [S5 +S6 +S8 +S9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC003 Other mutations of terminal turns stay refused by the REPL and plugins. requestClass-only updates and review writes are the exception: the REPL, plugins, and server accept them on terminal turns (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-004-AC004). [S6 +S8 +S9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC004 Item DELETE on a review section returns 400. [S4 +S5]

FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003 Per-status field validation.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003-AC001 `Unnecessary`/`Unstarted` carry no data fields. [S1]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003-AC002 `Running` requires `reviewer` and forbids scores. `Running` may carry `reviewedAt` and forbids scores, `reasons`, `otherNotes`, and receipt paths; a write with status omitted and fewer than two scores is rejected with `review_field_required_for_status` naming the missing score; `agentReview` `Pass`/`Fail` may carry receipt paths. [S1]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003-AC003 `Pass`/`Fail` require `correctness`, `completeness`, `reviewer`, `reviewedAt`; `hostileReview` `Pass`/`Fail` also require both jsonl paths. [S1]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003-AC004 `Other` requires `otherNotes` with at least one non-whitespace character (rejected otherwise with `review_other_notes_required`); scores, `reasons`, `reviewer`, `reviewedAt`, and receipt paths are optional. [S1]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003-AC005 `otherNotes` with text is rejected unless status is `Other`. [S1]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003-AC006 Scores lie in [0,1]; `reviewer` <= 256 chars; paths <= 2048 chars; `reviewedAt` needs an explicit offset and is stored UTC; reason items are non-empty. Each violation returns exactly one code from the wire-contract error list, identical on REST, MCP, client, REPL, and both plugin cores (golden contract). The field-to-code map is: `reviewer` over 256 characters or a receipt path over 2048 characters gives `review_field_too_long` naming the field and limit; `reviewedAt` without an explicit offset or not a valid ISO-8601 instant gives `review_timestamp_invalid`; an empty or whitespace-only reason item gives `review_reason_blank`; each is asserted with the same code and HTTP 400 by the golden contract in C#, Pester, and jest (`GoldenContract_FieldToCodeMap_AllSurfaces`: C# in S1, REST/MCP/client in S5, REPL in S6, Pester in S8, jest in S9). [S1 +S5 +S6 +S8 +S9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003-AC007 Validation runs before any mutation on every surface. [S4 +S5 +S6]

FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-004 Lossless decimal scores.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-004-AC001 `0.1234567890123456789` and `0.1234567890123456789012345678` round-trip unchanged through every hop: client JSON [S2], SQLite/PostgreSQL/SQL Server storage [S3], REST and MCP [S5], REPL YAML quoted/unquoted and JsonElement [S6], PowerShell plugin YAML block/flow and JSON [S8], Node core and TypeScript hosts [S9].
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-004-AC002 A JSON number or numeric string is accepted when its raw text matches `^[0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)?$` (no sign, no exponent) with a value in [0,1]; a normalized number (trailing fractional zeros stripped) is returned. [S1 +S2]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-004-AC003 Validation runs on the raw lexeme before any numeric conversion, so nothing is ever rounded: more than 28 fractional digits is rejected with `review_score_precision` (e.g. `0.12345678901234567890123456789`, which `JsonElement.TryGetDecimal` would round), exponent or signed forms with `review_score_format`, and values outside [0,1] with `review_score_out_of_range`; each case is tested as a JSON number, a JSON string, an unquoted YAML scalar, and a quoted YAML scalar. [S1 +S5 +S6 +S8 +S9]

FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-005 Hostile validation receipt mapping.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-005-AC001 `accuracy/100` maps to `correctness` and `completeness/100` to `completeness` exactly. [S8 +S9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-005-AC002 The plugins send the receipt's verdict as `hvVerdict` (AGREE or DISAGREE) with the mapped scores and no status, and the server maps it atomically at that write with the thresholds in force for the turn's workspace (including per-instance overrides): AGREE stores the derived Pass or Fail; DISAGREE stores Fail when the scores derive Fail, otherwise `Other` with a nonempty `otherNotes` chosen deterministically: the failed claims (FAIL entries of `reasons`) when present, otherwise the UNKNOWN claims, otherwise the verdict-level reason `DISAGREE with no listed failed or unknown claims` plus the response receipt path (`HvVerdictMapping_DisagreeUnknownOnly_PassScores_OtherNamesUnknownClaims`, `HvVerdictMapping_DisagreeNoListedClaims_PassScores_OtherVerdictLevelReason`). No client reads thresholds, and a mapped receipt never returns `review_status_conflict` (`HvVerdictMapping_InstanceOverride095_Scores097And099_AgreeStoresPass_DisagreeStoresOther`). `hvVerdict` travels unchanged through the client and the MCP tool (`HvVerdict_ClientAndMcpTool_ForwardedToServerMapping`, S5) and the REPL validator, schema, and flat-arg allow-list (`ReplRecordReview_HvVerdict_ValidatorSchemaFlatArgs_Forwarded`, S6). `hvReceiptPath` is owned by the plugin verbs (PowerShell S8, Node S9): the plugin reads and maps the receipt and sends the mapped fields with `hvVerdict`, and never forwards `hvReceiptPath` to the REPL, MCP tool, client, or server. [S4 +S5 +S6 +S8 +S9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-005-AC003 `reasons` (each FAIL and UNKNOWN claim of the receipt, prefixed `FAIL:` or `UNKNOWN:`), the plugin sends `hvVerdict` AGREE only when the receipt's OverallVerdict is AGREE and every claim is PASS, otherwise DISAGREE (`HvReceipt_AgreeWithFailOrUnknownClaim_SentAsDisagree` in S8, `hvReceipt_agreeWithFailOrUnknownClaim_sentAsDisagree` in S9), `reviewedAt`, `reviewer` come from the receipt, and a DISAGREE receipt with empty FAIL and UNKNOWN lists and no `reasons` is valid input; the Codex HV receipt (the reviewer's JSON receipt and the `--json` response jsonl) is accepted as input. A receipt is refused by the plugin before any write with one code from the wire-contract error list naming the field: a verdict that is not AGREE or DISAGREE with `review_invalid_status` (field `hvVerdict`), a missing accuracy or completeness with `review_field_required_for_status` (field `correctness` or `completeness`), an accuracy or completeness outside 0..100 with `review_score_out_of_range`, and a jsonl path that does not exist with `review_receipt_paths_required` (field `requestJsonlPath` or `responseJsonlPath`) (`HvReceipt_Invalid_RefusedBeforeWrite_ExactCodes` and `HvReceipt_DisagreeUnknownOnly_EmptyReasons_OtherNotesNamesUnknownClaims`, `HvReceipt_DisagreeNoListedClaims_EmptyReasons_OtherNotesVerdictLevelReason` in S8, `hvReceipt_invalid_refusedBeforeWrite_exactCodes` and `hvReceipt_disagreeUnknownOnly_emptyReasons_otherNotesNamesUnknownClaims`, `hvReceipt_disagreeNoListedClaims_emptyReasons_otherNotesVerdictLevelReason` in S9); the server stores receipt paths as text without checking that they exist. [S8 +S9]

FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-006 Review status lifecycle.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-006-AC001 Status accepts the six values case-insensitively and returns PascalCase. [S1]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-006-AC002 `hostileReview` flips `Unstarted` -> `Unnecessary` when the class becomes Chore, and back when the class changes away while still `Unnecessary`. The flip and its reverse apply only to in-progress turns; a class change on a terminal turn never changes either review record. [S1 rule, S4 service]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-006-AC003 Records past `Unstarted`/`Unnecessary` are never changed automatically. [S1, S4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-006-AC004 Clients cannot write `Unnecessary` or `Unstarted`. The only client path that yields those statuses is a section DELETE or a PUT turn omitting the review, which resets to the default (FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC004), or a requestClass change (AC002); a review body naming either is rejected with `review_unnecessary_not_client_writable`. [S1 +S5]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-006-AC005 Clients may write `Running`, `Pass`, `Fail`, `Other` from any state, including reruns. [S1]

FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-007 Configurable pass thresholds.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-007-AC001 `Mcp:SessionLog:ReviewThresholds:{Agent|Hostile}:{Correctness|Completeness}` with per-instance override; default 0.98 for all four. [S4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-007-AC002 Pass iff both scores meet their thresholds (inclusive, exact decimal). [S1]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-007-AC003 An omitted status is derived; a supplied `Pass`/`Fail` that contradicts the derivation is rejected with 422 `review_status_conflict` and details. [S1 +S4 +S5]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-007-AC004 `Other` is never derived. [S1]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-007-AC005 Thresholds in force are stamped on `Pass`/`Fail` records and are read-only. Changing thresholds never re-derives or restamps an existing record; a later write to that record derives and stamps with the thresholds in force at that write. [S4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-007-AC006 Startup and `./build.ps1 ValidateConfig` reject thresholds outside [0,1]. [S4]

FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-008 All agent plugins expose class and reviews.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-008-AC001 The Node core provides `requestClass`, `recordReview`, decimal-string scores, the completion pre-check, and HV mapping. [S9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-008-AC002 cline, cline-v2, and opencode adopt them and pass their suites. [S9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-008-AC003 The six PowerShell hosts receive the synced core with updated skill docs and pass their suites. [S8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-008-AC004 One golden contract file `docs/context/session-turn-review-contract.json` drives the C#, Pester, and jest rule tests. Its plugin pre-check cases include the unsupported-session failsafe close and the advisory behavior (warns with the missing items, never refuses) of FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-004-AC002, run by Pester and jest only (the server has no host session state). [S1 +S8 +S9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-008-AC005 The feature matrix (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC006) also lists `requestClass`, `recordReview`, exact scores, and the completion pre-check per plugin, with any documented limitation. [S11]

### New technical requirements (23), each with ACs

- TR-SUPPORT-CORE-016 Turn replace and section service (created in P0, same style as TR-SUPPORT-010E/010F). `ac-1` `ReplaceTurnAsync`, `ReplaceTurnSectionAsync`, `ClearTurnSectionAsync`, `DeleteTurnItemAsync` in `SessionLogService` with `TurnSection`/`ParseSection` (`SessionLogService.cs:1287-1317`) are the single implementation behind the REST, MCP, and client surfaces [existing; P0]; `ac-2` `TurnSection` gains `AgentReview` and `HostileReview` with the FR-SUPPORT-016 `ac-5` aliases, where replace maps to the review write path and clear resets to the default status, refusing a completed or failed turn with `review_section_reset_on_terminal_turn` and no mutation [S4]; `ac-3` item delete on a review section raises a validation error mapped to 400 [S4 service, S5 surfaces].
- TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001 Turn-open lock, ingress mint client, system-event classifier (lib-ps). AC-001 `turn-open.lock` serializes opens across processes in one workspace cache; AC-002 `Get-PluginIngressNonce -Host <host> -Payload <payload>` derives the nonce from the event exactly as the host binding table states for each hook host (a pure function: equal payloads give equal nonces), and when a payload lacks the field its host row names, the hook does not invent a nonce: it reports `turn-open-failed` with reason `nonce_source_missing` and writes a failsafe diagnostic, which the stop gate shows as warning `nonce_source_missing`; the nonce is written into the failsafe mint record before the first mint call, and the host binding table equal-field rule is applied under `turn-open.lock` before that write (`indistinguishable_arrival`); AC-003 classifier returns true exactly for the FR-002 event list; AC-004 hook-originated verbs carry `MCP_PLUGIN_HOOK_INVOCATION=1`; AC-005 `Invoke-PluginIngressMint` calls the server mint with the nonce, records the returned requestId (a prompt-hook mint is delivered at once, so it also becomes the host session's write target; a bridge mint stays `queued` until delivery), and on outage opens `pending-<nonce>` and rebinds it on drain through the C2 alias journal `sessions/<key>/aliases.yaml` (one atomic replace adding `{ pendingId, requestId, aliasedAtUtc } plus the root-id envelope of FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC009` before any queued record is rewritten; readers resolve either id through it; replay reapplies a journaled alias without minting); AC-006 `Start-PluginArrivalBridge` (claude-code, claude-cowork) is launched by SessionStart as a detached `pwsh -NoProfile -NonInteractive` process with the workspace root as working directory, writes `sessions/<key>/arrival-bridge.pid`, tails `transcript_path` from `appliedThroughOffset`, applies each `queue-operation` record by the D5 lifecycle in the host binding table (every `enqueue` takes a queue position, system events as unminted `kind: system` entries; mint at a user `enqueue`, deliver at `dequeue` and `absorbed_mid_turn`, withdraw at other `remove` and `popAll`) to `turn-state.yaml` in one atomic replace that also advances `appliedThroughOffset`, skipping records that start below it, exits at SessionEnd or when its host session is ended, and a second start for the same key exits immediately; `Sync-PluginArrivalLog` (also called by the Stop hook and SessionStart, TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-003 AC-007) records the transcript length when a session-log verb starts and, after the verb takes `turn-open.lock`, applies only complete records that end at or before that length before the verb binds (records appended later are left for later invocations; `Binding_DequeueAppendedAfterStartBeforeLock_BindsToEarlier`), and on claude-code and claude-cowork the prompt hook neither mints nor claims. A4 extends AC-006 with the verb-side halves of the bridge cases, and A5 extends AC-005 with the rebind of real queued writes through the persist path. [A3 +A4 +A5]

`ingressNonce` is the bound turn's nonce) once per verb invocation into its persist and failsafe records; AC-007 `sessions/<hostSessionKey>/turn-state.yaml` (Node: `turn-state.json`) is written only by the arrival bridge, or a verb, the Stop hook, or SessionStart applying pending transcript records through `Sync-PluginArrivalLog` up to the transcript length at its own start (the Stop payload carries `transcript_path`; and `sessions/<hostSessionKey>/ended.yaml` (`{ reason: session_end | idle, endedAtUtc }` with the session-level envelope) only by the session-end hook, the 12-hour idle rule, and the revival of an idle-ended session (removal, atomic under `turn-open.lock`).
